Company - Management

❌ D4711 questions
C-5554C-5650C-5752C-57b48C-5844C-5946C-6056C-6145C-6230C-6355C-6447

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Management is stable, competent, honest and conservative, and has shown in 2026 that it can act decisively when the opportunity is unambiguous. It is not a management that sets outlier standards, that compounds capital, or that has delivered the growth it has promised; the record over five years is of good restructuring, disciplined pricing in acquisitions, and a business that grew more slowly, earned less on its capital and converted operating improvement into EPS less reliably than the plans said. The governance is clean and the communication is straightforward, which makes the company easy to model, but the thin ownership, the unnamed succession for a CEO whose term ends in 2027 and the decentralised model that limits what the centre can change are structural ceilings on what the Consumer should expect management to deliver. The right modelling stance is that management will hit the ranges it guides within a year (it has a sandbagging habit and raises when it can), will under-deliver on the multi-year plan (revenue and acquisition targets have been missed in each of the last three years), and will protect the balance sheet before it protects growth. Treat the 2030 targets as the ceiling of the cone, not its centre.

C-55

Size of ambition or vision, particularly relative to peers

C54

Question asked How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

Judgement. The ambition is moderate-to-large on paper (revenue from EUR 1.74bn to about EUR 3bn by 2030, adjusted EBITA from EUR 148m to over EUR 330m, EPS from EUR 2.07 to over EUR 4.50, EUR 500m of acquisitions, international expansion) and moderate in practice, because the pace of delivery and the willingness to stretch the balance sheet are both conservative. Relative to peers: more ambitious than Gesco, less than Storskogen's 15% EBITA CAGR or Sdiptech's 15% growth and SEK 2bn EBITA ambition, and of a different kind from Lifco or Indutrade, whose ambition is expressed through sustained deployment rather than stated targets. The 2026 tungsten decision is the one act of real ambition in the record: management chose to carry the industry's inventory to win share rather than to protect cash, which is the behaviour of a company that wants to win. The history of missed revenue targets (the legacy EUR 2bn by FY25; FY23, FY24 and FY25 initial guidance) says the ambition has outrun the delivery, and the Business Foundations report's calculation that the 2030 target implies a 73% revenue increase that places the ambition in the top 5% of German industrial holdings is, by the same token, a warning about credibility.

C-56

Quality of the strategy: widening the moat, growth as priority, dynamic across environments

C50

Question asked How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

Judgement. The strategy is sensible, well structured and not bold. Its three pillars (acquisitions, internationalisation, engineering competence) address the group's three real problems (slow organic growth, German concentration, technological obsolescence risk) and the two-tier M&A design, the technology fields and the willingness to divest are improvements on the prior framework. It widens the sourcing moat (reputation grows with each permanent-ownership deal) but does not create any new moat, because it leaves the operating model (decentralisation, no central purchasing, no synergy) untouched. Growth is now a stated priority, which is new. The strategy is adaptive to environments in one respect (it funded BETEK through a shock that would have broken a less liquid owner) and rigid in another (it cannot run the M&A programme and a working-capital shock at once, and it cannot recycle capital out of weak subsidiaries at any speed). The Risks report's view that the hold-and-develop model creates permanent capital lock-up and eliminates rebalancing flexibility is the strategic weakness the 2030 plan does not address.

C-57

The chief executive, judged by actions

C52

Question asked How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

Judgement. Dr Johannes Schmidt is a competent, long-tenured engineer-operator who has done two decisive things well (the automotive exit in 2023, taking a EUR 124m FY22 loss to remove a permanent drag; the 2026 decision to fund BETEK's supply and capture share) and has otherwise managed a conservative, slow-moving holding with a mixed record against its own targets. He is an enabler rather than an ossifier, but not an animal: acquisitions have been disciplined (5.5 to 6.5x) and under-budget rather than aggressive; organic growth has been negative on his watch for three years; ROCE fell from 10.8% to 8.5% between FY23 and FY25; and he holds 6,200 shares (0.02% of the company) after twenty years on the board. The Leadership report's verdict is a disciplined operator with strong agency, medium plausibility on execution, and the outlier-standards marker as the most fragile dimension. The caveat the Leadership report itself raises, that the 2026 turnaround may be late-cycle luck, is fair: the tungsten outcome depended on a Chinese policy decision, and the courage was in financing it, not in creating it. His contract runs to December 2027, within the Consumer's forecast horizon, and no successor is named.

C-57b

Priority on hiring well, and the quality of the leadership team

❌ D48

Question asked How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

Judgement. The team is stable, experienced and engineer-heavy (four of five Management Board members are engineers), with a strong CFO in Rudolf Weichert (20 years at KPMG, 14 years in post, heads risk and compliance, placed an oversubscribed EUR 125m promissory note, steered leverage within policy through the shock). Segment heads are long-tenured (Meyer since 2017, Großmann since 2019) with Gudrun Degenhart recruited externally for Materials in 2023 from thyssenkrupp and ISS. Below the board, the evidence of hiring as a priority is thin: a 45-person holding adding four people for M&A, digital and ESG; 63 lean managers qualified; an IR head replaced without explanation; no disclosed succession for either the CEO or CFO; the Nomination Committee not meeting in FY25. The decentralised model means the leadership that matters is the 43 subsidiary managing directors, about whom nothing is disclosed except that founders are retained; Storskogen's 54% subsidiary CEO turnover is the cautionary peer. Key-person risk is mitigated by a deputy regulation and segment COOs but not eliminated: the sourcing network is Schmidt's.

C-58

How well resourced and invested the whole business is (the buffer; the ant colony)

❌ D44

Question asked How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

Judgement. Under-invested relative to plan and relative to the strategy's demands, with a lean central buffer. Property and equipment investment was EUR 60.6m in FY25 against a planned EUR 75 to 85m, and FactSet capex has run at 2 to 3% of sales (EUR 40 to 51m on fixed assets) against D&A of EUR 90m-plus, which is a pattern of harvesting rather than building; consensus has capex rising to EUR 74 to 88m from FY26, which the Consumer should check against the company's own statements. The innovation budget (EUR 3.3m through the Innovation Development Bank) and the Sustainability Development Bank are small. The holding has 45 people for 43 companies in 180 entities in a dozen countries, which the Earnings Review flags as a span-of-control risk as European and US hubs multiply. Resilience was demonstrated in one respect: the balance sheet had the liquidity (EUR 218m of cash at year-end 2025, undrawn lines) to fund EUR 165m of working capital in a quarter without breaching policy, which is the "ant colony built yesterday" quality the question asks about. Workforce resilience is weaker: trainee ratios fell (3.7% to 3.2%), skilled-labour shortages are a named standing risk, and the employee share plan was cancelled.

C-59

Holistic and congruent culture, aligned across goals, strategy, execution and decisions

❌ D46

Question asked How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

Judgement. The culture is congruent with the old model (permanent ownership, entrepreneurial subsidiaries, light-touch holding, 25 years of dividends, low turnover, long tenures) and only partly congruent with the new strategy. The 2030 plan asks for EUR 600m of acquired revenue, European and US hubs, technology fields, lean programmes and divestments, which requires more central capability and more intervention than a 45-person holding that "intervenes when it hears discords" has historically exercised. The compensation reform aligns pay with the 2030 EPS goal, which is congruent; the absence of equity ownership at board and employee level, the cancelled ESOP and the decentralised HR model are incongruent with a Lifco-type owner-operator culture the Leadership report uses as the benchmark. The Avanza or Ryanair standard (goals, strategy, culture, execution and decisions all pulling one way) is not met; the culture is coherent but passive, and the decisions that matter are made subsidiary by subsidiary.

C-60

Clarity, straightforwardness and helpfulness of communication, including the driver transmission

C56

Question asked How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

Judgement. Communication is clear, honest about the big things and inconsistent about the medium things. Honest: management said in plain terms that the Materials Solutions development is "unique and exceptional", not the "new normal", that margins will subside, that inventory would be written down if prices fell, and that the working-capital decision was deliberate. Clear: segment-level revenue and margin guidance, order intake and backlog by segment, a 24-month backlog disclosure, book-to-bill, explicit free-cash-flow and leverage targets, and ad-hoc releases when guidance changes. Inconsistent: guidance has moved five times in two years, EPS is never guided, the capex plan was missed without much explanation, the IR head changed without comment, and the driver transmission from the tungsten price to segment margin (contract lag, inventory turnover, hedging) has not been quantified by the company at all, which is why the sell-side and this analysis have to borrow Sandvik's three-month lag. The memo's own grade for conservative investor relations (B) is fair; the Q1 review's comment that the cash-flow trade-off was under-communicated (no timetable for inventory monetisation) is the gap the Consumer most needs filled at the Q3 report.

C-61

Record of acting, not just talking: recent huge delivery and mid-term track record

❌ D45

Question asked How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

Judgement. The record is of acting in bursts and delivering below plan in between. Acted: the automotive exit (completed 2023), the five-to-three segment consolidation, the counter-cyclical buyback (2.0m shares, 1.1m retired), the 2026 supply decision (and its payoff: adjusted EBITA +120% in H1), the first disposal under the new policy, two acquisitions in H1 2026. Delivered below plan: revenue missed initial guidance in FY23 and FY24 and the legacy EUR 2bn FY25 target; acquisitions have run at half the budget in two of the last three years; acquired EBITA undershot its target; Engineering has not reached its margin band since FY24; organic growth negative three years running; free cash flow guidance cut 100% in 2026 even as profit guidance rose 45%. Against guidance and estimates the record is covered under C-69; beyond it, the mid-term picture is a company that executes the restructuring it decides on and then grows more slowly than it says it will, in every environment, which is a reliability of a kind but not the "huge delivery" the question seeks.

C-62

Outlier standards: driving higher, faster and better; out-executing competitors

❌ D30

Question asked To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

Judgement. No. Every measurable standard sits at the sector median or below: ROCE 8.5% against 14 to 25% at the compounders it would like to be compared with; cash conversion cycle 203 days against about 80 at Lifco; organic growth below the sector median; Engineering margin 5.6% against a 7% sector median; working capital at 30-plus percent of sales against 15 to 22% at peers; capex below plan. The one place Indus out-executed competitors in the last eighteen months (BETEK's supply security) was a decision about financing capacity, not about operating standards. The Leadership report names outlier standards as the most fragile dimension of the management assessment, and the Change Forces report's own weak-signal list (Engineering margin, organic growth, working-capital intensity, German concentration) is a list of standards not met. The lean-manager and AI programmes show intent; the numbers do not yet show results.

C-63

Shareholders first, ahead of management; prudence of governance

C55

Question asked Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

Judgement. The governance is prudent and clean, and the shareholder orientation is conventional rather than exemplary. Evidence for shareholders first: an uninterrupted dividend for over 25 years, a 40 to 50% payout policy applied consistently (47.7% for FY25), counter-cyclical buybacks at a low multiple, no capital increase planned and none needed, disciplined acquisition pricing, a single share class with one vote each, a 100% independent supervisory board with 50% female representation, clawbacks and share-ownership guidelines from 2026, related-party transactions of EUR 832k, zero loans to management, a clean audit, an expanded internal audit function, and pay that actually fell when EBIT missed its target (the 2025 bonus factor dropped; the 2022 long-term tranche paid 54% because the stock lagged the SDAX; pre-2020 options expired worthless). Evidence for management first or for softness: change-of-control severance of up to two years' pay; Welcker's re-election beyond the 15-year tenure guideline; a CEO pay ratio rising from 18 to 24 times; board and management holding under 0.1% of the shares combined with no open-market buying; the cancelled employee share plan; and the practical point that the working-capital decision, while defensible, transferred a policy risk to shareholders' balance sheet without a stated exit plan. The scaffolding is in place; the skin in the game is not.

C-64

Enlightened capital allocator and dynamic repurchaser, with a record of high RONIC

❌ D47

Question asked To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

Judgement. A disciplined allocator, not an enlightened one. Discipline: never overpays (5.5 to 6.5x EV/EBIT; under-spends budgets in weak markets), funds from cash flow and long-dated debt, holds leverage in a narrow band, pays a steady dividend, bought back shares counter-cyclically when the stock was at about 7x earnings. Not enlightened: the capital deployed earns 8 to 11% because the model holds everything forever, including businesses that dilute returns (the Risks report's "inelastic asset base"), so the high return on new invested capital from each acquisition never lifts the group return; the buyback was modest (EUR 5m in 2025) and has stopped; the dividend is paid out of a working-capital-intensive business at a payout ratio that leaves less for the growth plan than the plan needs; and the largest capital allocation of the decade (EUR 165m into tungsten inventory) was a reactive, one-off, externally driven bet rather than a repeatable discipline. The recent record of acquisition returns (acquired EBITA below target; Gestalt's negative contribution; IMECO's closure) is mixed. The Consumer should model the allocation as policy-driven and steady (dividend at 40 to 50%, M&A at what cash flow allows, no buybacks while the share price is above the five-year multiple median), not as a source of EPS accretion.

Risks

❌ D324 questions
C-8044C-8134C-8233C-8328

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The risk profile is dominated by one mechanism with three expressions: a temporary, policy-created price in one subsidiary has been capitalised by the market as a run rate (sentiment risk), has been financed with the group's own balance sheet (fundamental risk through inventory value and leverage), and has lifted guidance and consensus to levels that only the persistence of the policy can sustain (estimate risk). The probability that at least one expression bites within 18 months is high, because normalisation is the base case of every source including management; the question is speed and depth, and the Q3 2026 report is the first observable test. Beneath this, the ordinary risks of a German industrial holding (recession, subsidiary failures, wage inflation, succession) are moderate and well disclosed. The skew of share-price outcomes over twelve months is negative; the fair trough is 20 to 35% below the current price on normalised earnings and a mid-cycle multiple, and the tail is deeper. The one risk that would make the picture better rather than worse is that the working-capital release arrives faster than the margin decline, which would improve cash and leverage while earnings fall; the Consumer should model that path explicitly because it is the most likely shape of FY27.

C-80

Deceleration of the core growth engine over twelve months: revenue growth below the company's normal, and the input force itself slowing

❌ D44

Question asked To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

Judgement. The core growth engine (organic volume) was not decelerating over the last twelve months; it was reaccelerating from a three-year contraction to low-single-digit growth, which is at or slightly above the company's normal rate (which, over the cycle, is German GDP). The headline engine (reported revenue +15% in H1, +21% in Q2) is running far above the company's normal rate because of price, and the input force behind that price (Western tungsten scarcity) is itself slowing: spot has softened about 15% from peak, Chinese domestic prices are half Western, Western supply is scheduled from 2027, management expects margins to subside in H2, and Sandvik and Kennametal both describe their tungsten benefit as front-loaded. The second input force (succession-deal sourcing) is not slowing structurally but has slowed in execution (deal flow decelerated by working-capital uncertainty). So the honest answer is: the real engine is slowly accelerating from a low base, the apparent engine is at peak and about to decelerate sharply, and the input to the apparent engine has already turned. The Consumer should expect reported revenue growth to fall from +16% in FY26 to about zero in FY27 (consensus +1.4%) with the underlying volume growth of 2 to 4% hidden inside it.

C-81

Fundamental risks that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability: plausibility and size

❌ D34

Question asked What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

Judgement. Four, in descending order of expected cost. (1) An abrupt lifting or loosening of Chinese tungsten export licensing, or a demand collapse at current prices: plausibility moderate (it is one administrative decision, and the Western-Chinese price gap is an incentive to relax it); magnitude very large (Materials Solutions margin from 22% to 8% or below, inventory valuation allowances on a share of EUR 495m-plus of inventory, group EPS below EUR 2.50 against consensus 5.21 for FY26 and 3.94 for FY27, net debt/EBITDA toward 3x on the fall in EBITDA). (2) A German industrial recession deepening rather than recovering under higher rates and energy costs: plausibility moderate (the Fitness for Fighting regime; 28% of SMEs expect deterioration); magnitude large (half of sales; Engineering already at 5.6%; the FY20 precedent of revenue -10.6%). (3) A failure or impairment at a single subsidiary (the Gestalt, IMECO, SMA, Schäfer and Selzer precedents; goodwill impairment of EUR 10.5m per 10% cut to segment plans; the insolvency administrator's lawsuit management classes as low-to-medium risk): plausibility moderate-to-high over any 18-month window; magnitude small-to-medium (EUR 5 to 30m). (4) A cyber event across increasingly networked subsidiaries: plausibility low-to-moderate; magnitude unknown; named by the company as a significant risk. The group's Monte Carlo puts maximum potential risk at EUR 76m at 98% confidence, which is a useful order of magnitude for (2) to (4) but does not capture (1).

C-82

Perception- or sentiment-driven risks that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere

❌ D33

Question asked What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

Judgement. The sentiment risks are at least as large as the fundamental ones and are more probable, because the share price has already moved: +69% over twelve months, +35% over three, at a 24-month-forward P/E of 8.5x that is at the 89th percentile of its own five-year range, with technical momentum rated A and relative strength in the 94th percentile. Specific channels: (i) a Chinese export-policy headline, which would hit the share before any number changed; (ii) read-across from Sandvik (reports 22 October; tungsten commentary), Kennametal and Ceratizit on tungsten pricing or front-loaded benefits; (iii) the regime: Fragility is Double Underweight in the Fitness for Fighting digest, small, leveraged, illiquid (USD 3 to 5m a day) names are named as what the market refuses, and the 2022 precedent is that compounders' multiples halved as yields rose; (iv) the sell-side turning on valuation, which has begun (NuWays to Hold on 6 October on valuation alone; buy share down 14 points in a month); (v) a German-sentiment or SDAX de-rating on a weak ifo or a political event; (vi) the Q3 print on 12 November, where any sign that working capital is not releasing or that Materials Solutions margin is falling faster than the 15 to 17% full-year range implies would be read as the false-friend thesis confirmed. The memo's own park verdict and the Risks report's hold-and-watch are the house's own sentiment, and the share has risen 20% since the memo without the memo's conditions having been met.

C-83

How far the shares could fall to a fair trough multiple

❌ D28

Question asked How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

Judgement. Far. The arithmetic has three layers. Multiple: the 24-month-forward P/E is 8.54x against a five-year median of 6.70x, a 25th percentile of 6.27x and a 10th percentile of 5.91x (ten-year dashboard bands: median 8.9x, 35th percentile 7.7x, 10th 5.5x). Earnings: the 24-month-forward consensus EPS of about EUR 4.37 blends FY26's 5.21 and FY27's 3.94; a normalised base is EUR 3.6 to 3.9 (NuWays and consensus FY27), and the Change Forces bear case is EUR 2.07 to 2.77. Price: EUR 37.05. Combinations: the five-year median multiple on normalised EPS of EUR 3.75 gives about EUR 25 (-32%); the 25th-percentile multiple on the same EPS gives about EUR 23.5 (-37%); the Risks report's 8x on EUR 3.65 gives EUR 29 (-22%); a crash multiple of 5.5 to 5.9x on bear-case EPS of EUR 2.5 gives EUR 14 to 15 (-60%). The share traded at EUR 20 to 21 as recently as the 52-week low, which is the market's own recent estimate of a trough. A fair trough on normalised earnings and a mid-cycle multiple is EUR 24 to 29, a fall of 20 to 35%; the tail is below EUR 20. The asymmetry is the point: the upside from here needs the windfall to persist and the multiple to hold at the top of its range, the downside needs only normalisation.

Guidance

❌ D486 questions
C-6850C-7050C-7154C-7256C-8544C-7344

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance is specific, honest and short-horizon: it tells the Consumer what the current year will look like with good accuracy once the year is half over, and it tells the Consumer nothing quantified about the mechanism that will dominate the next year. The company's behaviour (sandbag, raise after delivery, cut promptly, never guide EPS) means that FY26 is likely to land in the upper half of the range and that FY27 guidance, when set in March 2027, will be conservative and below the current consensus trajectory for the 2030 plan. The informal commentary already points there. The Consumer's model should use guidance as the FY26 anchor, should build FY27 from the normalisation mechanics rather than from any company statement, and should expect the gap between guidance and consensus to open in the sell-side's disfavour at the FY26 report.

C-68

Company guidance against sell-side consensus for this year and next, and why

C50

Question asked To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. For FY26, consensus sits inside guidance, slightly above its midpoint on profit and at its midpoint on revenue; there is no gap to exploit. For FY27, the company gives no guidance, and consensus models a sharp normalisation that is in the direction management has signposted but not necessarily to the level management would privately expect. The reason consensus is aligned for FY26 is that management guides late and specifically (the 29 July raise came after Q2 was known), and the reason there is no tension for FY27 is that nobody, including the company, has quantified the unwind.

Reasoning. FY26 guidance: revenue EUR 1.90 to 2.10bn (midpoint 2.00bn); adjusted EBITA EUR 220 to 260m (midpoint 240m, margin 11 to 13%); Materials Solutions margin 15 to 17%, Engineering 8.5 to 10.5%, Infrastructure 10 to 12%; free cash flow at least break-even. Consensus (FactSet median, seven brokers): revenue EUR 2,016m; EBITDA EUR 315m; EBIT EUR 215m (which, adding back PPA amortisation of roughly EUR 21 to 25m, implies adjusted EBITA of about EUR 236 to 240m, the guidance midpoint); EPS EUR 5.21 (range 4.29 to 5.54); free cash flow EUR 32m (above break-even). NuWays, the most detailed broker, has adjusted EBITA of EUR 246.8m. So consensus is at or marginally above the midpoint on profit. The one place consensus is more optimistic than guidance is cash: EUR 32m against "at least break-even" is a small gap in the same direction. For FY27, consensus revenue EUR 2,043m (+1.4%), EBITDA EUR 292m, EBIT EUR 180m, EPS EUR 3.94 (range 3.63 to 4.99), free cash flow EUR 131m; the only company anchor is the 2030 target path (11% margin, EPS above EUR 4.50), against which FY27 consensus at an 8.8% EBIT margin is below trend, which is appropriate given the one-off. The implied H2 2026 in guidance (adjusted EBITA EUR 96.5 to 136.5m against EUR 123.5m in H1) is the useful detail: consensus at the midpoint is assuming H2 roughly 5% below H1, which is a modest normalisation; a faster unwind would take FY26 below consensus even while staying inside guidance.

C-70

The record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance

C50

Question asked Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

Judgement. Management is a tactical sandbagger that sets conservative initial ranges and raises them when delivery allows, and that cuts promptly when the environment turns; the raises come after the fact (Q1 2026 after Q1 was known; July 2026 after the Q2 preliminaries), not in anticipation. The record over three years is bidirectional and externally driven: FY23 revenue missed its initial range; FY24 guidance was cut in July (macro) and November (impairments) and then met on the revised basis; FY25 revenue guidance was cut in May (tungsten controls, in the opposite direction from 2026) and met; FY25 free cash flow beat its EUR 90m target by 38%; FY26 was raised twice by a cumulative 45% on the EBITA midpoint while free cash flow guidance was cut to break-even. The nature is therefore honest and reactive rather than confident and forward-looking: the company does not pre-announce improvement it has not yet booked, and it does not resist cutting. For the Consumer this means guidance is a reliable floor within the current year and a poor guide to the next, and that the next move in guidance (FY27, in March 2027) is more likely to be set low than high given the normalisation management has already signposted.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five, adjusted for the change in input forces

C54

Question asked How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

Judgement. For FY26, guidance is modestly conservative: the midpoint implies H2 adjusted EBITA 5% below H1 and a Materials Solutions margin in H2 of roughly 9 to 12% (to average 15 to 17% for the year from 22.4% in H1), which assumes a meaningful fade; given that APT has softened only about 15% from peak and Western prices remained above USD 3,100 in August, the fade may be slower than guided, which is upside within the year. For FY27 and beyond, the only guidance is the 2030 target set, which is aggressive rather than conservative against history: an 11% margin and EPS above EUR 4.50 against five years in which the margin peaked at 10.1 to 10.8% and EPS never exceeded about EUR 4.10 on the Viewforth definition. Adjusting for input forces: the last five years included a pandemic (FY20), a boom (FY21 to FY22) and a German recession (FY23 to FY25); the next three include the unwind of the largest positive input shock the company has had, a possible German fiscal recovery from 2027, and a higher-rate regime. On that adjustment the FY26 range is appropriately cautious and the 2030 path is optimistic; the Consumer should treat a FY27 initial guidance set in March 2027 at an 8 to 10% margin as the likely conservative anchor.

C-72

Specificity of guidance: built from stated building blocks or vague

C56

Question asked How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

Judgement. Specific at the segment and cash-flow level, vague on the one mechanism that drives the current year. Building blocks given: group revenue and adjusted EBITA ranges, a group margin range, three segment revenue directions and three segment margin ranges, free cash flow, capex planning ranges (EUR 75 to 85m for FY25), leverage policy, payout policy, the 2030 bridge (EUR 700m organic, EUR 600m acquired), the acquisition budget, backlog and book-to-bill by segment. Not given: EPS, tax rate, interest cost, PPA amortisation, the price-volume split beyond the two-thirds-one-third statement, the contract lag on tungsten pass-through, inventory turnover, any timetable for working-capital release, or the normalised Materials Solutions margin after the windfall. The result is that the Consumer can model revenue and EBITA from guidance with reasonable confidence and must build the EBITA-to-EPS and EBITA-to-cash bridges from first principles, which is where the model's error will sit.

C-85

Informal or qualitative commentary against formal guidance and consensus, and why

❌ D44

Question asked To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. Informal commentary is more cautious than the formal guidance and more cautious than consensus for FY27. Management's qualitative statements (the development is "unique and exceptional"; it "does not represent a permanent new normal"; segment margins "will subside over the year"; high prices and renewed Chinese competition could dampen demand; inventory would be written down if prices fell abruptly; the working-capital increase goes "well beyond normal seasonality") all lean toward normalisation and risk. The October conference feedback relayed by NuWays adds that deal flow has slowed and the strategic focus will return to M&A "once the situation is under control", which is a caution on the inorganic bridge. The one qualitative positive (BETEK "permanently solidified" customer relationships and captured share) is unquantified. Why the gap: management is managing expectations down ahead of a visible margin decline, consistent with its sandbagging habit, and the sell-side, having just doubled FY26 estimates, has had less reason to pre-empt the FY27 cut. For the Consumer: the qualitative signal is that FY27 consensus EPS of EUR 3.94 has downside, not upside, from the company's own perspective.

C-73

Anything else material or uncommon about guidance or investor relations

❌ D44

Question asked What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

Judgement. Five items. (1) The frequency of guidance changes (five in two years) is roughly ten times the German mid-cap holding norm the Risks report cites (0.3 to 0.5 revisions a year), which the market has learned to price as volatility. (2) The company guides adjusted EBITA (before PPA amortisation and impairments) and the brokers model it, but FactSet consensus is on EBIT and EBITDA, so the Consumer has to reconcile three profit definitions; the FY25 gap between adjusted EBITA (EUR 147.8m) and reported EBIT (EUR 127.0m) is the reconciliation. (3) Coverage is nine brokers in total but seven on FactSet, all German boutiques and mid-tier houses (NuWays, Parmantier, mwb, Metzler, Oddo BHF, MP Capital/Warburg, Pareto; LBBW and Kepler Cheuvreux historical), with no expert-call transcripts available in the research pool; consensus is therefore shallow and NuWays-led, and a single broker's model change moves the mean. (4) The IR head changed in 2026 without explanation. (5) The Q3 report on 12 November 2026 and the FY26 preliminaries in February 2027 are the two dated disclosures on which the whole case turns, and the company has not said what it will disclose on inventory monetisation.

Sell-side estimates

❌ D363 questions
C-6740C-6944C-7428

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell-side has done the obvious and done it late: it doubled FY26 estimates after the company told it to, rolled valuation to FY27 to avoid capitalising the peak, and modelled a normalisation that still leaves FY27 to FY28 profit EUR 40 to 50m above the pre-windfall run rate on an unproven assumption that BETEK keeps a double-digit margin. Breadth is complete (all brokers Buy bar one valuation Hold), revisions are at their highs, and coverage is shallow and led by one boutique, so consensus can move on one model change. The record in comparable conditions says the company misses initial targets in German downturns; the current environment is a German downturn with a temporary offset. The Consumer should treat consensus FY26 as reliable (it is guidance), consensus FY27 EPS of EUR 3.94 as the top of a EUR 3.0 to 4.0 range, and consensus FY28 as a placeholder that assumes the acquisition engine is running at a pace it has not run at since FY22. The direction of the next revision cycle is down, and the Q3 report on 12 November 2026 and the FY26 report in March 2027 are its dates.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, line by line, and how credible the gap is

❌ D40

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement. The sell-side has modelled FY26 as the windfall year and FY27 to FY28 as a partial normalisation to a level materially above the pre-windfall base, and the credibility of that higher level is the central estimate question. Line by line, consensus against history:

Revenue: FY26 EUR 2,016m (+16%), FY27 2,043m (+1.4%), FY28 2,158m (+5.6%); history FY21 to FY25 compounded at about 1.5% a year and FY23 to FY25 was flat to down. The FY26 number is anchored by guidance; FY27 assumes the price unwind is offset by volume and acquisitions; FY28 assumes the acquisition engine is running. Credible in direction; FY27 has downside if the unwind is faster.

EBITDA margin (brokers' basis): FY26 15.6%, FY27 14.3%, FY28 13.4% against FY25 12.8% and a FY23 brokers'-basis level of about 14.1%; EBIT: FY26 EUR 215m (10.7%), FY27 180m (8.8%), FY28 187m (8.7%) against FY25 137m (7.9%) and a FY21 to FY23 range of 10 to 10.6%. The FY27 to FY28 EBIT margin of 8.7 to 8.8% is below the FY21 to FY23 peak and above FY24 to FY25; it is credible only if Materials Solutions settles at 10 to 12% rather than 8 to 10%, Infrastructure holds 11%, and Engineering recovers toward 8%. The broker dispersion for FY27 EBITDA (EUR 247m at Pareto to EUR 310m at Oddo) is the honest measure of how uncertain that is.

EPS (brokers' basis): FY26 EUR 5.21, FY27 3.94, FY28 4.15 against FY25 2.96, FY24 2.37, FY23 2.29. FY27 at 3.94 is 33% above FY25, which requires the structural step-up above plus a tax rate near 30% and interest flat; FY27 broker range is 3.63 to 4.99.

Free cash flow: FY26 EUR 32m, FY27 131m, FY28 136m against FY25 72m (FactSet definition) or 124m (company). The FY27 to FY28 level assumes a working-capital release of around EUR 100m and capex rising to EUR 75 to 88m (against EUR 48m in FY25), which is internally consistent and the most credible line.

Other lines: D&A EUR 83 to 87m (plausible), dividend EUR 1.75 then 1.50 then 1.60 (policy-consistent), net debt implied to fall through FY27 to FY28, book value per share from about EUR 30 to 38 (consistent with retained earnings).

Credibility of the gap: the sell-side is right that FY26 is a peak and right that FY27 falls; the risk is that the normalised level it has chosen (EBIT margin 8.7 to 8.8%, EPS 3.9 to 4.2) embeds roughly EUR 40 to 50m of EBIT above the FY24 to FY25 run rate that depends on BETEK retaining margin, which the Kennametal and Sandvik precedents and management's own words both argue against. A normalised Materials Solutions margin of 9% rather than 11 to 12% would take about EUR 10 to 15m off FY27 EBIT and EUR 0.30 to 0.45 off EPS; a faster price fall with inventory write-downs would take more. The Consumer should carry a FY27 EPS range of EUR 3.0 to 4.0 with consensus at the top of it.

C-69

Record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over one, three and five years, including in comparable conditions

❌ D44

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

Judgement. Over one year: large beats. Q1 2026 adjusted EBITA EUR 42.5m against NuWays' EUR 24.7 to 25.7m (a 65 to 72% beat); Q2 2026 revenue EUR 523.6m against EUR 486m (+7.7%) and adjusted EBITA EUR 81.1m against EUR 37.6m (+116%); Q2 EPS EUR 1.85 against EUR 1.91 (a slight miss on tax and interest); FY25 adjusted EBITA ahead of expectations and revenue in line. Over three years: a mixed record skewed to misses in the downturn. FY23 revenue below the initial EUR 1.9 to 2.0bn range; FY24 guidance cut twice (the cumulative EBIT revision of EUR 25 to 35m, 15 to 20% of the initial range, against a 5 to 10% norm) and then met; FY25 revenue guidance cut in May and then met, adjusted EBITA met within its cut range, free cash flow beat by 38%. Over five years: FY20 was a pandemic loss year; FY21 to FY22 beat on the recovery; the pattern is that in comparable conditions (German industrial weakness: FY20, FY23 to FY25) the company misses its initial targets and meets its revised ones, and in strong conditions (FY21 to FY22, 2026) it beats. The 2026 beats are not evidence of improved forecasting ability because they came from the same unforecastable external force as the 2025 cut. Against the current environment (German stagnation, rising rates), the comparable-conditions record argues for initial FY27 guidance that is conservative and for the risk of a cut if the macro does not improve.

C-74

Have estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts; has guidance been rebased lower, kitchen-sinked or sandbagged

❌ D28

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

Judgement. The opposite on every count. Estimates have been raised, not cut: FY26 EPS consensus is up 43% over three months, 60% over six and 58% over twelve; FY27 EPS up 9%, 14% and 15%; FY28 up 8% and 18%; sales up 4 to 7%; EBITDA up 5 to 9%; target prices up 17% over three months and 26% over six. Ratings: 86% buy on FactSet (six of seven), mean rating 1.14, mean target EUR 42.29 against a price of EUR 37.05 (median 41.00); the only move in the last month is one downgrade to Hold on valuation (buy share -14 points), with no sell ratings anywhere. Guidance has been rebased higher, not lower, and the one element that was rebased lower (free cash flow to break-even) is the one the sell-side is already modelling above. There is no kitchen-sinking; the sandbagging is of the ordinary within-year kind. From the standpoint of this question, which seeks the contrarian setup of fully cut estimates and rebased guidance, Indus is at the wrong end: estimates are at their highs, breadth of upgrades is complete, and the next material revision is more likely to be down (FY27 at the FY26 report) than up. The Wisdom Library's entry on composite signals masking component deterioration applies in reverse: the revision-momentum score of 11 and the B rating for sell-side momentum are built on a one-off, and the dashboard will not distinguish the fade until it happens.

Stock market narrative

❌ D381 question
C-2242

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative has run ahead of the staircase. The market has paid for the windfall (a 69% twelve-month return, relative strength in the 94th percentile, a multiple near the top of its own five-year range), the sell-side has fully upgraded, and the dated positives that remain are mostly confirmations of what guidance already says, with the one genuinely novel positive (the working-capital release) arriving at the same time as the one genuinely novel negative (the margin unwind) and in the same report. The 2030 narrative is intact but unproven and its first real test is eighteen months away. For the Consumer, the narrative implication is that the model's FY27 earnings decline will be the market's first new information, and that the valuation case from here depends on the market choosing to capitalise normalised cash flow (EUR 130m a year, a 13% free-cash-flow yield on the current market capitalisation) rather than peak earnings; the sector regime currently refuses that kind of patience to small acquirers.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments ahead: within six months, months six to eighteen, months eighteen to thirty-six; novelty and foundation

❌ D42

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Judgement. The staircase is short, mostly already known, and several of its steps are double-edged. The market has had its novel positive (the tungsten windfall and two guidance raises) and the share price has consumed it; what lies ahead is the test of whether the windfall was a step up or a spike.

Within six months (to April 2027). (i) The Q3 2026 report on 12 November 2026: the positive version is working capital beginning to release, free cash flow positive for a second consecutive quarter, Materials Solutions margin holding in the mid-teens, Engineering margin recovering toward 8%, and a sign that deal flow has restarted; novelty moderate (the cash release would be new evidence), foundation good (Q2 already showed EUR 37m of positive free cash flow). (ii) The FY26 preliminaries in February 2027 confirming adjusted EBITA at or above the EUR 240m midpoint and free cash flow above break-even; novelty low (in guidance), foundation good. (iii) A FY26 dividend proposal around EUR 1.75 (consensus), a 35% rise; novelty low, foundation good at a 34% payout. (iv) Resumption of acquisitions (two or three Tier-2 bolt-ons, possibly a Tier-1 platform) once working capital is "under control"; novelty moderate, foundation moderate (NuWays dates it to that condition). (v) The US defence procurement restriction on Chinese tungsten from 1 January 2027, supportive of Western prices; novelty low, foundation good but the effect on BETEK's prices is indirect. (vi) A further small disposal or two under the new policy; novelty low. (vii) German fiscal stimulus beginning to convert into Infrastructure orders; novelty moderate, foundation weak (ZDB data say not yet).

Months six to eighteen (April 2027 to April 2028). (i) The FY26 annual report and initial FY27 guidance in March 2027: the likely content is a margin range of 8 to 10% and a cash-flow target well above FY26, which is positive for cash and negative for earnings narrative; novelty moderate. (ii) FY27 free cash flow of EUR 100m-plus from working-capital release, net debt falling toward 2x, equity ratio back toward 40%; novelty moderate, foundation good if tungsten stabilises. (iii) Engineering backlog conversion through 2027 (the US plant-engineering projects) lifting the segment toward its 8.5 to 10.5% band; novelty moderate, foundation moderate. (iv) Acquired-revenue contribution rising toward 4 to 5% as the 2030 programme catches up; foundation weak given the record. (v) A possible capital markets update on 2030 progress; management's contract extensions mean no leadership catalyst before the end of 2027, at which point the CEO succession question itself becomes an event. (vi) Western tungsten supply (Sangdong) coming online in 2027: negative for price, positive for BETEK's supply security and feedstock cost; double-edged.

Months eighteen to thirty-six (April 2028 to October 2029). (i) The 2030 target path: by FY28 the company needs to be visibly on the way to EUR 3bn and 11% for the targets to be credible; the positive version is acquired revenue compounding at 5 to 7% and the margin at 10%; novelty moderate, foundation weak-to-moderate. (ii) EPS above the FY25 base on a normalised basis (consensus FY28 EUR 4.15), which would prove the windfall left a higher base; foundation moderate. (iii) CEO succession resolved (the current term ends December 2027); novelty high, direction unknown. (iv) A take-private or strategic approach if the shares de-rate back to 6 to 7x on normalised earnings while private multiples stay at 8 to 12x; novelty high, probability low.

Overall: the next six months contain one genuinely new positive (cash release) and several confirmations; the following year contains the earnings normalisation that the narrative has not yet had to face; the third year is where the 2030 story either becomes investable or is quietly reset. The staircase does not support a further re-rating from an 8.5x 24-month-forward multiple at the 89th percentile of its own range.

Back to contents

The Investment Analyst's closing notes for this layer

Overall judgement across all groups

Indus Holding is a solid, honestly run, modestly profitable German industrial holding whose normal economics (organic growth at German GDP, through-cycle adjusted EBITA margins of 8 to 10%, ROCE of 8 to 11%, heavy working capital, good cash conversion, comfortable leverage) do not compound, and which in 2026 has booked the largest profit in its history from a policy-created tungsten scarcity captured by one subsidiary at the cost of carrying the industry's inventory on its own balance sheet. The foundational components for the Consumer's model are therefore: a Materials Solutions price-and-margin unwind from Q2 2026's 26.9% toward 8 to 12% over four to six quarters, timed by Chinese export policy, Western supply and APT spot, with inventory valuation as the tail risk; low-single-digit organic volume growth in Engineering and Infrastructure with Infrastructure's digital-network demand and Engineering's 2027 to 2028 project deliveries as the identifiable positives and German rates and wages as the identifiable negatives; a working-capital release of EUR 100m or more that repairs cash flow, leverage and the equity ratio through FY27 regardless of earnings; an acquisition engine that is paused and will restart at the pace the balance sheet funds (2 to 4% of revenue a year, not the 2030 plan's 6 to 7%); and an EBITA-to-EPS bridge (PPA, interest, a tax rate of 30 to 35%, a flat share count) that has historically absorbed much of the operating improvement. Consensus FY26 is guidance and reliable; consensus FY27 to FY28 sits at the top of the credible range; the share price sits at the top of its own multiple range on peak earnings in a regime that is refusing re-ratings to small leveraged acquirers. The investment case as it stands is a well-evidenced false friend with a clean balance sheet exit: the numbers will get better on cash and worse on earnings at the same time, and the Consumer's model should show both.

Score: 42

Request: T-P3

Why requested: The INDUS case contains two feedback loops whose direction will decide the next 18 months of cash flow and earnings: a reinforcing loop in which BETEK's supply security won share and pricing that funded more inventory and more share, which now risks running in reverse as prices fall and inventory is written down, and a balancing loop in which the working capital absorbed by that trade has starved the acquisition programme on which the 2030 targets depend. Focus: What most needs answering is whether BETEK's share and pricing loop retains any permanent gain when tungsten normalises, and how quickly the working-capital release allows INDUS to restart the acquisition programme, since those two loops set both the FY2027 earnings floor and the pace at which the 2030 revenue target can be approached.
