What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Third and fourth sets of questions: Indus Holding (INH-DE), as at 2026-10-24

Preface: what the Consumer should know before reading

Four points frame every answer below.

First, the three gaps in the Financial Analyst's model summary, and what they mean. The one-page summary carries three places where the model and the second set's Analyst differ, and the Consumer should understand each before using the model's figures.

(1) The earnings-per-share gap (model 5.48, 3.88, 4.49, 4.94, 5.44 against the Analyst's 5.42, 3.80, 4.37, 4.86, 5.38; gaps of EUR 0.06 to 0.12) is the minority interest the model does not carry (the summary says minorities are not modelled, by Richard's ruling of 16 October 2026). The Analyst deducted minorities of EUR 1.2m, 1.5m, 2.0m, 2.0m and 2.5m, which is EUR 0.05, 0.06, 0.08, 0.08 and 0.10 a share; the rest is rounding. No forecast input is wrong; the model's EPS is simply before minorities, and the Consumer should deduct about EUR 0.06 to 0.10 a share when quoting it.

(2) The free-cash-flow gap (model -8.6, 182.0, 111.8, 105.1, 114.4 against the Analyst's 6, 190, 120, 114, 125; the model is EUR 8 to 15m lower every year) is almost exactly PPA amortisation multiplied by the tax rate: EUR 7.3m, 7.5m, 7.9m, 8.0m and 8.1m for FY26 to FY30. The forecast-inputs form takes depreciation excluding PPA amortisation, so the model's pre-tax profit is before PPA and the 34% tax rate is charged on a profit about EUR 22 to 26m higher than the profit the company is actually taxed on (acquired intangibles are tax-deductible at the limited partnerships, as the tax report confirms). The model therefore overstates the cash tax charge by EUR 7 to 8m a year, or about 4% of free cash flow and about EUR 35m of net debt by FY30. The further EUR 7m of gap in FY26 is the Analyst's assumption that FY26 cash tax reflects FY25's low book tax (which contained the EUR 8.36m SMA credit); the further EUR 2.5m in FY30 is small and probably the re-levering interest on acquisitions. I have not changed the tax-rate input to compensate, for one reason: the correction would be to lower the model's rate to about 30.5%, 28.9%, 28.2%, 27.5% and 26.8%, which would raise the model's EPS (already before PPA and so EUR 0.58 to 0.74 above the brokers' basis) by a further EUR 0.30 a share and inflate the total shareholder return the model computes on that EPS. The input is right on its own definition (the brokers' basis); the model applies it to a different base. The Consumer should treat the model's net debt as EUR 8m a year too high, cumulatively, and its free cash flow as EUR 8m a year too low, and leave the EPS as it is.

(3) The phasing note ("FY2026: Part 3's EBITDA margin by period and Part 1's margin for the year differ; the periods were moved by -2.9 points to match the year") is a basis problem, not an input problem, and it matters for the next 18 months. The Analyst's Part 1 (15.7% for FY26) and Part 3 (15.7% for Q3 2026, 13.8% for Q4) are internally consistent on the brokers' basis: H1 2026 adjusted EBITA of EUR 123.5m plus about EUR 37m of depreciation is EUR 160.5m (16.6% of H1 revenue), and adding Q3 at 15.7% of about EUR 500m and Q4 at 13.8% of about EUR 538m gives EUR 313m for the year, which is 15.6%. The model, however, holds the H1 2026 actual on FactSet's operating EBITDA basis (EUR 76.5m plus EUR 116m, EUR 192.5m, 19.9% of revenue), which is EUR 32m above the brokers' basis. To reach the year's 15.7% from that higher H1, the model needed H2 EBITDA of only EUR 122m (11.8% of H2 revenue) against the Analyst's intended EUR 153m (14.7%); the difference is exactly 2.9 points. Two consequences. The model's annual figures for FY26 (EBITDA 314.7, EBITA 238.7, EPS) are right and on the brokers' basis. The model's quarterly figures for Q3 and Q4 2026 are wrong by about 2.9 points of margin (too low), so the Consumer must not use the model's quarterly margins as the test for the 12 November report; the tests are the Analyst's own quarterly adjusted EBITA figures (Q3 about EUR 59.5m, Materials Solutions about 17.5%; Q4 about EUR 55m, Materials about 11%), which I use throughout. The model's quarterly growth rates for 2027 against those quarters will be correspondingly distorted. The Financial Analyst should move the H1 2026 actual to the brokers' basis rather than touch the inputs.

Second, the model's return at the guideline fair multiple is the largest single inconsistency in the files, and the Consumer should resolve it in topics #11 and #12. The model's total shareholder return of +99.5% over 18 months is 98.9 points from the multiple, which the guideline sets at 14.26x (the ten-year 90th percentile, the Master Dashboard's "mania" band) against 8.7x today, and it is calculated on the model's pre-PPA EPS (the 24-month-forward figure at December 2027 is about EUR 5.19 a share, giving EUR 74.0). The first set judged the fair multiple at the company's own history, 7 to 9x, and I agree, for reasons set out under D-07 and the fourth set. On the brokers'-basis EPS that the market actually capitalises (the mean of FY28 EUR 3.73 and FY29 EUR 4.17, about EUR 3.95 at December 2027), the arithmetic is: at 14.26x, EUR 56 (not 74); at the ten-year 75th percentile of 11.4x, EUR 45; at the ten-year median of 8.9x, EUR 35; at today's 8.5x, EUR 34; at the 35th percentile of 7.7x, EUR 30. With the FY26 dividend of EUR 1.90 paid in the window, the 18-month total return is therefore about 0% at the ten-year median multiple, -4% at today's multiple, -13% at the conservative band and +57% at the guideline band. The model's +99.5% requires both the mania multiple and the pre-PPA EPS basis; neither is defensible for a company whose earnings fall 30% in the window. I return to this in the fourth set's judgement.

Third, where I disagree with the earlier sets. I agree with the second set's central path and have changed no forecast input. I disagree, or add, in four places. (i) The first set expected FY26 in the upper half of guidance and the second set moved to the midpoint; the October peer evidence (Sandvik's Q3 on 22 October still carried a positive tungsten timing benefit of SEK 104m and its customers accepted September price increases; Kennametal's benefit is front-loaded into the September and December 2026 quarters; Western APT has plateaued near its high while only Chinese domestic prices have collapsed) says Q3 2026 is more likely above the Analyst's path than below it, so the FY26 risk is to the upside within guidance while the FY27 risk is to the downside. The midpoint remains the right central estimate because management itself guided an H2 fade, but the skew of the two years differs and I say so under I-01. (ii) The second set said consensus FY27 free cash flow embeds no working-capital release; that is right for the FY27 line (consensus operating cash flow of EUR 206m against EBITDA of EUR 292m less about EUR 74m of tax and interest leaves no room for a release), but the consensus FY26 line (free cash flow EUR 32m) is NuWays' figure and NuWays' model embeds a EUR 104m working-capital reduction in H2 2026 and a further EUR 39m in FY27, a cumulative EUR 143m against the model's EUR 26m in H2 2026 plus EUR 80m in FY27, EUR 106m. The cash surprise against consensus is therefore mostly one of phasing and of a stale median, not of a release nobody expects, and it may arrive earlier (at the 12 November report) and smaller in FY27 than the model shows. (iii) The second set's FY28 to FY30 path (acquired EBITA of EUR 12 to 16m a year, margin drifting to 9.7%, EPS compounding at 9% from FY25) leans toward the constructive end of the first set's management judgement ("will under-deliver on the multi-year plan"); it is a defensible base but the Consumer should carry FY29 and FY30 as the upper half of the cone rather than its centre, because the acquired-EBITA assumption is six to eight times the EUR 1.9m the company delivered against an EUR 8 to 9m target in FY25. (iv) On price retention at BETEK, the pricing-power evidence is more adverse than the second set's "half in FY27": the company's own annual report says BETEK adjusts prices "at short intervals" to pass on raw-material costs, which is the index-linked wear-parts model (Kennametal's Infrastructure segment, where prices adjust downward automatically as APT falls) rather than the catalogue cutting-tool model (Sandvik, Kennametal Metal Cutting, three-to-nine-month lags, prices retained). The second set's 10% FY27 segment margin is therefore the middle of a range whose lower half (NuWays' 8.8%) is at least as likely as its upper half; the effect is about EUR 3 to 8m of FY27 EBITA, inside the cone, and I have not changed the input.

Fourth, scores. For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the item is for the predictability of the forecasts over the next 18 months (100 most positive, 1 most negative, 50 neither). For the fourth set, the score says how favourable the gap is between our assessment and what guidance, sell-side estimates and the narrative already reflect (100 most favourable, 1 least, 50 neither).

The third set: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks

❌ D427 questions
D-0168D-0258D-0335D-0435D-0548D-0646D-0738

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The predictability of INDUS's financial forecasts over the next 18 months divides into a direction that is about as certain as forecasting allows and a magnitude that is about as uncertain as a company of this size and disclosure can make it. Both earlier sets, every covering broker that has updated since October, the company's own guidance and management's own words agree that adjusted EBITA falls from about EUR 238m in FY26 toward the high EUR 100s in FY27 while working capital releases and leverage holds at 2.0 to 2.2x; the two sets are congruent on this to a degree that leaves no analytical dispute for the Consumer to adjudicate. The uncertainty is concentrated in one subsidiary, one input price and one policy variable, and in three coefficients (BETEK's repricing interval, its cost-flow lag and the value of its inventory) that the company does not disclose and that have to be borrowed from Sandvik and Kennametal; that concentration gives a FY27 EPS cone of about EUR 2.5 to 3.8 around a base of EUR 3.2, roughly symmetric around the model, negatively skewed against a consensus that is stale at EUR 3.94, and positively skewed on cash against a consensus that has not modelled the release in FY27. The forecast relies on demonstrated strengths for the next 18 months (within-year guidance reliability, balance-sheet discipline, Infrastructure's mix, BETEK's commercial execution) and on an undemonstrated one (acquisition deployment at scale) only beyond them. Disclosure is good at segment level and silent on BETEK, so the case will be monitored by inference from peers and from the APT price, with the Q3 report on 12 November, Kennametal's call on 4 November, the February preliminaries and the March 2027 guidance as the four events that narrow the cone from about EUR 75m of EBITA to perhaps EUR 30m by April 2027. The share price is a separate matter: it has decoupled from its peers upward on the one-off (84% idiosyncratic variance, 35% six-month return against 5% for the basket) at a multiple at the 89th percentile of its five-year range, so the predictability of the share price is lower than that of the enterprise value, and the model's return at the guideline multiple of 14.26x on pre-PPA EPS (+99.5%) is not a forecast the Consumer should carry; on brokers'-basis EPS at the company's own median multiple the 18-month return is about zero. For an investment case over the next 18 months, the forecasts are predictable enough to say with confidence what will happen and not predictable enough to say by how much, and what will happen is a fall in the line the market has capitalised.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

C68

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement. Highly congruent on the mechanism, the direction and the shape of the next 18 months, with the disagreements confined to timing and to the level of two lines, and with one serious incongruence that sits not between the two sets but between both sets and the Financial Analyst's model summary (the return at the guideline multiple). The two sets were written three days apart from overlapping evidence and both arrive at the same five-part architecture: a policy-created tungsten windfall in one subsidiary that reverses from Q4 2026 through H1 2027; a working-capital release that repairs cash and leverage while earnings fall; a through-cycle margin band of 9 to 10% that neither cost action nor pricing power nor any feedback loop lifts; an acquisition engine that is sound, paused and restarts at a pace below the 2030 plan; and a conservative, honest, sandbagging management whose guidance is a reliable floor within the year and silent on the next. Congruence of that kind is positive for predictability, because it means the forecast does not depend on resolving a dispute between the two analyses; the residual uncertainty is in the world, not in the reasoning.

Reasoning, with the points of tension named.

The alignments that matter. The first set's external-forces group (score 45), its economic-strength group (40), its risks group (32), its guidance group (48) and its sell-side group (36) all point to the same base case the second set then quantified: FY26 at the guidance midpoint (adjusted EBITA about EUR 238m), FY27 at about EUR 174m (8.8%), free cash flow near zero in FY26 and about EUR 190m in FY27, leverage at 2.0 to 2.2x throughout. The first set's instruction to "model two cycles out of phase" (C-06) is exactly what the second set's P-09 delivers ("earnings and cash move in opposite directions in FY27"). The first set's "treat the 2030 targets as the ceiling of the cone" (Management group) is the second set's FY30 margin of 9.7% against the 11% target. The first set's fair-multiple anchor of 7 to 9x (C-50, C-83) is consistent with the second set's statement that the share "has capitalised earnings at the top of its own multiple range". The AlphaSense reports for this set corroborate rather than disturb: the plausibility report (T-D1) rates the 2030 transition "plausible only under optimistic scenarios", the concentration report (T-D2) finds the forecast "heavily concentrated on BETEK's tungsten windfall", the guidance report (T-I1) finds FY27 consensus "stale" and downward revisions "highly probable", and the narrative report (T-I2) finds the second-order points "overlooked". No report found evidence that overturns a judgement in either set.

The tensions, in descending order of consequence.

(1) The model's return. Both sets judge the fair multiple at the company's history (median 8.9x on ten years, 6.7x on five); the model computes the return at 14.26x and on pre-PPA EPS, which produces +99.5% in 18 months, 99% of it from the multiple. This is not a disagreement between the sets; it is the guideline multiple applied mechanically to a stock whose phase (peak earnings, about to fall) is the opposite of the phase the mania band describes. The Consumer must address this directly in topics #11 and #12; the preface gives the arithmetic.

(2) FY26 upper half versus midpoint. The first set's "modestly conservative" guidance (C-71) and the second set's "midpoint, not above" (P-06) differ by about EUR 8 to 10m of adjusted EBITA (NuWays' EUR 246.9m against the Analyst's EUR 238m). The October peer evidence favours the first set's direction for Q3 2026 (Sandvik and Kennametal still booking timing benefits; Western prices plateaued, not fallen) and the second set's direction for Q4 2026 and H1 2027 (Chinese domestic APT down 69% from the March peak; Chinese finished tools being pushed into Europe; Sangdong in commercial production). The two are reconcilable: FY26 lands in the upper half of a range whose midpoint is the prudent forecast, and FY27 falls harder than the stale consensus. The tension is about one quarter's timing and does not change the shape.

(3) The working-capital release and consensus. The first set thought consensus FY27 free cash flow of EUR 131m embedded a EUR 100m release; the second set showed it embeds none. Both are partly right, because the consensus median mixes NuWays (which embeds a EUR 104m release in H2 2026 and EUR 39m in FY27) with five stale models from July and August that embed little. The point for the Consumer is that the cumulative release NuWays expects over 18 months (EUR 143m) is larger than the model's (EUR 106m) but earlier; the model may therefore be too cautious on FY26 cash and too generous on FY27 cash, with a similar total. The 12 November working-capital figure decides the phasing.

(4) Management delivery versus the FY28 to FY30 path. The first set's management group says the company grows more slowly than it says it will in every environment and has delivered acquired EBITA of EUR 1.9m against a target of EUR 8 to 9m; the second set's base case has acquired EBITA of EUR 12 to 16m a year from FY28 and EPS compounding at 9% from FY25. These are congruent only if the balance-sheet release is the sole reason for the under-delivery, which is the second set's implicit claim (the pause was "a choice within policy", so the restart is faster than Storskogen's or Sdiptech's). The T-D1 report's finding that the deal-budget under-spend predates the tungsten shock (EUR 35m of EUR 70m in FY24) argues that sourcing capacity, not only cash, limits the pace. I treat FY29 and FY30 as the upper half of the cone rather than its centre; this does not affect the 18-month forecasts.

(5) Price retention at BETEK. The first set (C-45, C-17) and the second set (P-04) both say BETEK keeps a quality premium and loses the scarcity premium; the second set quantifies retention at half of the 2026 price gain in FY27. The pricing-power report's finding that BETEK reprices at short intervals (the company's annual report) and that index-linked wear-parts pricing adjusts downward automatically (Kennametal, May and August 2019) places BETEK closer to the fast-give-back end of the industry than the second set's precedents (Sandvik's and Kennametal's metal-cutting retention) imply. This is a difference of degree inside the second set's own stated range (a quarter to two-thirds retained) and it skews FY27 slightly down.

(6) Two minor jars. The first set carried a FY26 dividend of EUR 1.75 (consensus) in its reasoning while the second set models EUR 1.90; the difference is EUR 4m of cash and immaterial. The first set's Infrastructure demand judgement is "the cleanest demand story in the group" while its C-33 notes distributor-mediated construction products are where margins are most exposed; the second set's 11.3% margin path treats digital-network mix as dominant; both can be true and the segment's H1 2026 delivery (11.2%, 12.6% in Q2) supports the second set.

Nothing in the earlier sets contradicts anything else on the central question of this set, which is whether FY27 earnings fall and cash rises. Every source, including management and the three most recently updated brokers, says yes.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and how far does the thesis rely on demonstrated strengths rather than strengths to be acquired?

C58

Question asked To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

Judgement. The expected change over the next 18 months is, unusually, mostly a reversal rather than an achievement, and reversals do not require strengths: the forecast that adjusted EBITA falls from about EUR 238m to about EUR 174m while free cash flow rises from about zero to about EUR 190m rests on the mechanics of a price unwinding and an inventory being sold, both of which the company and its peers have demonstrated in the same cycle running the other way. The parts of the forecast that do require strengths are four, and they divide cleanly: two rest on strengths the business has shown (Infrastructure holding 11% on digital-network mix; the balance sheet absorbing and then releasing a EUR 165m working-capital swing within policy), one rests on a strength it has shown intermittently (Engineering at 8.5 to 9% through backlog conversion, which it did in H2 2025 and FY25 but not in H1 2026), and one rests on a strength it has not shown (BETEK retaining a double-digit margin as a non-integrated toolmaker after supply normalises). Beyond 18 months, the four-year path relies on one further strength the company would need to acquire, which is deploying EUR 75 to 95m a year of acquisitions at 6 to 7x when it has never deployed its own budget in full. The thesis for the memo, which is about the next 18 months, is therefore plausible on demonstrated delivery; the thesis for years three and four is plausible only if the sourcing engine scales, which is unproven.

Reasoning, strength by strength.

What the record demonstrates. The company met or beat its revised guidance in each of FY23, FY24 and FY25 and beat its FY25 free-cash-flow target by 38%; it has paid a dividend for over 25 years; it funded a EUR 165m working-capital build in two quarters without breaching its 2.5x leverage policy and turned operating cash flow positive (EUR 44.6m) in the very next quarter; Infrastructure lifted its margin from 9.1% to 11.2% through cost optimisation and mix while residential construction fell for a fourth year; BETEK passed through a tripling of feedstock twice and gained share. These are the strengths the 18-month forecast uses: within-year guidance reliability (FY26 at the midpoint), cash discipline (the release), Infrastructure resilience (11.3%), and BETEK's commercial execution (a quality premium of a few points above the FY24 to FY25 margin of 8.8 to 9.4%).

What the record does not demonstrate. (i) Engineering at its band: 9.2% in FY25 and 11.7% in H2 2025, but 5.6% in H1 2026 against a 1.22x book-to-bill, with the record backlog concentrated in 2027 and 2028 US plant projects. The Engineering report's peer evidence is uncomfortable: Dürr's systems business converts in 6 to 24 months, KION needed three years to flush un-indexed legacy backlog, Krones cut its 2026 and 2028 targets on 22 October 2026, Bertrandt warned on postponements in 2024, Jenoptik lost 200 basis points to under-absorption; capacity utilisation in the segment is 79% against a historical 85%, and the company's own annual report says cost inflation "could not always be passed on quickly or fully". The model's 8.5% for FY26 requires a Q4 at about 12.5%, which the segment has delivered in Q4 2025 but which depends on project completions landing in the quarter. Medium plausibility, with the downside the more likely miss. (ii) BETEK at 10% in FY27: the plausibility report asks directly whether a non-integrated carbide toolmaker has ever retained a material part of a scarcity-driven gain and finds no clean precedent; the retention cases (Sandvik, Kennametal) are integrated or catalogue-priced, and the 2015 and 2019 precedents for index-priced wear parts are immediate give-back with a two-to-three-quarter cost lag. The second set's half-retention is a judgement about quality and relationships, not a demonstrated outcome. Medium-low plausibility; the range 8.8 to 11% is the honest statement. (iii) The acquisition engine at EUR 55 to 60m in FY27 and EUR 75 to 95m a year from FY28: the company spent EUR 35m of EUR 70m in FY24, EUR 16.6m of EUR 50m in H1 2026, delivered EUR 1.9m of acquired EBITA against EUR 8 to 9m targeted, and pays 7 to 7.5x on recent deals against the 5.5 to 6.5x it cites; the Nordic peers that do deploy EUR 100m-plus a year (Lifco 17 deals in 2025, Indutrade nine in H1 2026) have organisations built for it and are now sourcing in Germany. The model's path is below the 2030 plan and is plausible in cash terms (the balance sheet will have the capacity from 2027), but the sourcing and execution capacity at that pace is the strength to be acquired.

What the forecast does not rely on, to its credit. No cost-out programme (P-03 assumes net wage inflation exceeds savings in FY27), no pricing power outside BETEK beyond cost recovery, no compounding loop, no re-rating, no central procurement, no recycling at scale, no CEO succession resolved. The forecast is built on the business as it is.

The one place the thesis needs something to go right rather than merely unwind is the shape of the FY27 trough: the model needs Engineering and Infrastructure to add about EUR 6 to 8m of EBITA together while Materials loses about EUR 85m, so that the group holds 8.8% rather than falling to 8%. That is low single digits of improvement in two segments that grew organically in H1 2026 and carry record order books; it is plausible on the record, and if it fails the cost is about EUR 0.2 of EPS, which is inside the cone.

D-03

How diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences between the forecasts and the last four to six years' financials?

❌ D35

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement. For the next 18 months the drivers are as concentrated as they can be in a 43-company holding: one subsidiary (BETEK, about 16% of group sales and about half of Materials Solutions), one input price (Western ammonium paratungstate) and one policy variable (Chinese export licensing) explain essentially the whole of the FY26 departure from history and the whole of the FY27 return toward it, on both the earnings line and the cash line, because the same inventory that generated the margin consumed the cash and will release it. Of the EUR 90m increase in adjusted EBITA from FY25 (EUR 147.8m) to FY26 (about EUR 238m), Materials Solutions contributes about EUR 84m; of the EUR 64m decline from FY26 to FY27, Materials Solutions contributes about EUR 70m with the other two segments adding about EUR 6m back. Concentration of this degree has two opposite effects on predictability. It makes the direction of change almost certain, because no source, including management, expects the scarcity premium to persist, and because the arithmetic of a price that falls faster than the cost of the inventory it was earned on does not depend on anyone's execution. It makes the magnitude and timing of the change unusually uncertain, because the three coefficients that set them (BETEK's repricing interval and formula, the cost-flow lag through its inventory, and the tonnage against value of that inventory) are undisclosed by the company and have to be borrowed from Sandvik and Kennametal. From FY28 the drivers diffuse into the ordinary ones of a holding (Engineering utilisation, Infrastructure demand, acquired mix, tax rate, wage inflation), each worth tens of basis points of margin a year, which are individually small, collectively slow and far more predictable.

Reasoning.

The decomposition. Against the FY21 to FY25 base (revenue EUR 1.63 to 1.80bn, adjusted EBITA margin 8 to 10.8%, brokers'-basis EPS EUR 2.3 to 3.9), the FY26 forecast differs by about EUR 270m of revenue and EUR 90m of EBITA. Price at BETEK is about EUR 130m of the revenue and the Materials margin going from 9.4% to 18% is about EUR 84m of the EBITA; inorganic growth adds about EUR 40m of revenue and EUR 3m of EBITA; Infrastructure's margin gain adds about EUR 6m; Engineering subtracts about EUR 4m; holding costs subtract EUR 2m. The FY27 forecast then differs from FY26 by about EUR -40m of revenue and EUR -64m of EBITA, of which Materials is about EUR -97m of revenue and EUR -70m of EBITA, wage inflation net of productivity about EUR -8m across the group, and Engineering, Infrastructure and acquisitions together about EUR +14m. Everything that is not BETEK nets to a few million either way.

The cash line is the same driver. Working capital rose EUR 165m in H1 2026 on the company's definition, of which NuWays attributes about EUR 48m directly to tungsten feedstock at Q1 and the rest to the price-inflated receivables, inventories and advance payments that followed; the FY27 release of about EUR 80m in the model is the deflation of those same balances. The concentration report's phrase is accurate: "the same inventory build was a deliberate strategic decision to capture market share", so earnings and cash are two faces of one bet.

The amplification. Group adjusted EBITA moves about EUR 10m, and brokers'-basis EPS about EUR 0.27, for each 10% of BETEK's 2026 price gain retained or lost in FY27 (second set, P-10). A EUR 20m inventory allowance rather than EUR 5m is EUR 0.40 of EPS. Those two sensitivities alone span EUR 1.2 of FY27 EPS, or about 35% of the base case, from one subsidiary. For comparison, the whole of Engineering's margin band (8.5 to 10.5%) spans EUR 13m of EBITA, and the whole of Infrastructure's (10 to 12%) spans EUR 13m.

Why this is worse than it looks for the FY27 figure and better than it looks for the direction. The company discloses segment revenue and adjusted EBITA quarterly, not BETEK's; it has never quantified the price-volume split beyond "about two-thirds price"; it has not disclosed the contract lag, the inventory tonnage or the coverage of its recycling; the AGM speech of 3 June 2026 revealed that major customers co-finance inventory (advance payments received rose from EUR 19.1m to EUR 34.5m) but not on what terms or for how long. Every number the model needs for the quarter-by-quarter path is a borrowed coefficient: Sandvik's one-month price lag and three-month cost lag in powder, three-month and nine-month in tools; Kennametal's two quarters of inventory and index-linked infrastructure pricing. Precedent for the two to four quarters after a tungsten peak shows a wide spread of outcomes for toolmakers (Kennametal's 360 basis point gross-margin drag in a single quarter in late 2019; Sandvik's 85% negative operating leverage in Q1 2013; Kennametal's Infrastructure operating loss in the December 2015 quarter after the Fanya collapse) and in every case the direction was the same. The concentration therefore gives a confident sign and an uncertain size.

Diffusion after FY27. From FY28 the model's drift of 20 to 30 basis points a year comes from five small levers (Engineering utilisation, acquired mix, lean and ERP programmes, disposals of underperformers, a tax rate falling a point a year) and the revenue growth of 5 to 5.5% from organic volume of 2 to 3% plus acquired revenue of 2 to 3%. These are many small drivers of the kind a diversified holding normally has, and they are predictable in aggregate to within a point of margin; their collective error is far smaller than one quarter of BETEK's price.

The peer control case confirms the reading: Gesco, the German holding without a tungsten effect, grew H1 2026 revenue 4.8% and EBIT 53% from a depressed base with a 5.1% return on sales; that is what INDUS's other 42 companies look like, and it is the predictable part.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

❌ D35

Judgement. The cone is wide for a company of this size and type, roughly symmetric around the model's base case on earnings, skewed negative against consensus on earnings, skewed positive against consensus on cash, and skewed negative on the share price because the multiple sits at the 89th percentile of its own five-year range at the top of the earnings cycle. On brokers'-basis FY27 EPS, the plausible range is about EUR 2.5 to 3.8 around a base of EUR 3.22, a width of about 40% of the base; on FY27 adjusted EBITA, about EUR 140 to 215m around EUR 174m; on FY27 free cash flow before acquisitions and dividends, about EUR 130 to 230m around EUR 190m. The width is set almost entirely by the three BETEK coefficients (price retention, cost-flow lag, inventory value) and is narrowed by four dated events inside the window: Kennametal's September-quarter call on 4 November 2026 (the closest read-across to BETEK's Q3), the INDUS Q3 report on 12 November, the FY26 preliminaries in February 2027 and the initial FY27 guidance in March 2027, by which point the H1 2027 contract round and the Sangdong ramp will be visible. The case is therefore predictable in direction (earnings down, cash up, leverage flat to down), unpredictable in magnitude for about two more quarters, and should become materially more predictable by March 2027. For an investment case that depends on the earnings line, that is a poor profile; for one that depends on the cash line, it is a fair one.

Reasoning.

The branches, with my probabilities. (1) Plateau extended (Western prices hold near USD 2,500 to 3,100 per MTU through the 2027 contract round because Chinese raw-material export restrictions persist, the US defence procurement rule from 1 January 2027 bids for traceable supply, the US Defense Logistics Agency's stockpile contract absorbs Western material, and Sangdong's ramp is slower than planned): BETEK retains two-thirds or more of its price, Materials Solutions holds 13 to 15% in FY27, group adjusted EBITA about EUR 200 to 215m, FY27 brokers'-basis EPS about EUR 3.8, working-capital release only EUR 30 to 40m, free cash flow about EUR 130m. Probability about 25%. This is the T-I1 report's "upside" and the NuWays view that "a rapid price decline is rather unlikely"; it is also the branch in which the share holds its multiple longest. (2) Base (Western prices fall toward USD 1,500 to 2,000 during H1 2027 as the Chinese domestic collapse transmits through finished tools and Sangdong supply arrives; BETEK retains about half of its price gain and a third of its volume gain; a EUR 5m allowance in Q4 2026): adjusted EBITA about EUR 174m, EPS about EUR 3.2, release about EUR 80m, free cash flow about EUR 190m. Probability about 50%. (3) Fast unwind (Chinese finished tools at a feedstock cost 40% below Rotterdam take volume as well as price; large OEM customers and Wirtgen dealers push back under framework agreements; an inventory allowance of EUR 15 to 25m in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027; Materials Solutions back to 8 to 9%): adjusted EBITA about EUR 140 to 155m, EPS about EUR 2.5 to 2.7, release about EUR 100m but partly consumed by the write-down, free cash flow about EUR 200m and net debt to EBITDA briefly toward 2.6 to 3x on the lower EBITDA. Probability about 25%. The expected value of EPS across the branches is close to the base, which is why I call the earnings cone roughly symmetric around the model and negatively skewed only against the stale consensus of EUR 3.94.

Why the cone is wider than the company's history suggests. In FY21 to FY25 group adjusted EBITA moved within EUR 138 to 195m on volume and mix; the FY27 branches span EUR 75m on one subsidiary's price. The concentration report's precedents for toolmakers after tungsten peaks show the same width: Kennametal's Infrastructure margin went from 58.4% (June 2026 quarter, with USD 198m of timing benefit) toward a guided mid-teens EBITDA margin exit for fiscal 2027; its 2019 quarter lost 360 basis points of gross margin on a USD 70 drop in tungsten; the Fanya collapse took APT from USD 340 to about USD 200 over three years and pushed Kennametal's Infrastructure into an operating loss within two quarters. For decentralised acquirers whose forecasts rested on one segment, the T-D2 report's examples (Storskogen, Sdiptech's elevator and electric-vehicle units, Dürr's BBS Automation) show forecast misses of 20 to 40% on the affected line.

What narrows the cone, and when. The Q3 2026 Materials Solutions margin (12 November) is the first observable: above 20% says the plateau branch is live and FY26 lands in the upper half of guidance; 16 to 19% is the base; below 15% says the unwind is a quarter ahead of the model. The working-capital balance at 30 September (company definition, against EUR 627m at June and EUR 461.5m at December 2025) tells the Consumer whether the release is front-loaded (NuWays' EUR 104m in H2) or back-loaded (the model's EUR 26m); a reading below EUR 580m favours the front-loaded path. Kennametal's 4 November call will give the first post-peak price and cost commentary from an index-priced wear-parts business. The February 2027 preliminaries will show any inventory allowance. The March 2027 guidance will reveal management's own FY27 margin range, which, given its habit, will be set at or below the base branch. By April 2027, the cone on FY27 EBITA should have narrowed from EUR 75m to perhaps EUR 30m. The CME tungsten futures contract due to start trading in early Q1 2027 will, for the first time, give a forward curve for the input price, which will make the H2 2027 path more legible than any prior cycle.

The skews that matter for the share price rather than the forecast. The share price has risen 69% in twelve months and 35% in six against a peer basket up 5%; the 24-month-forward P/E of 8.5x is at the 89th percentile of five years; NuWays moved to Hold on valuation on 6 October and Pareto's target (EUR 37) is at the price. In the plateau branch, the share probably holds EUR 35 to 42 through the winter; in the base branch, the FY27 earnings cut and the March guidance take the multiple toward its median (EUR 28 to 35); in the fast-unwind branch, the share revisits EUR 24 to 29 on normalised earnings and a mid-cycle multiple (first set, C-83). The cash-flow branch is positively skewed in all three cases except the plateau, and in the fast-unwind branch the cash arrives while the write-down lands, which is the configuration in which the market punishes earnings and ignores cash. The predictability of the share price over 18 months is therefore lower than the predictability of the enterprise value, which the two lines largely offset.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers for monitoring the case over about the next six months?

❌ D48

Question asked How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

Judgement. Helpful at the segment level and nearly silent at the level of the one driver that matters. The company gives quarterly segment revenue, adjusted EBITA and margin, order intake, backlog and book-to-bill by segment (a 24-month rolling disclosure introduced in Q2 2024), working capital on its own definition, net financial debt, free cash flow on its own definition, the leverage ratio, segment margin ranges and a group range for the year, and it issues ad-hoc releases when guidance moves; that is above the German mid-cap norm and it is why FY26 can be modelled to within a few percent. It does not give, and has said nothing to suggest it will give, BETEK's revenue, volume, price, inventory tonnage, inventory value, contract repricing interval, the share of sales under framework agreements, the duration of the customer co-financing arrangements, the tax rate, the interest cost, PPA amortisation, acquisition multiples, or any timetable for inventory monetisation; the cash-flow statement is published half-yearly so the Q3 cash figures are the company's summary lines and FactSet derivations. For the next six months the monitoring of the case must therefore run on three company numbers (the Q3 and Q4 Materials Solutions margin, the quarter-end working-capital balance, and any inventory allowance) and on a set of external proxies the Consumer must assemble (Western and Chinese APT prices and their spread, Sandvik's and Kennametal's quarterly pricing and timing commentary, Almonty's Sangdong ramp, the CME futures curve from Q1 2027, VDMA orders and German capacity utilisation, the ifo index, and the ad-hoc deal announcements). That is workable but it is monitoring by inference, and it means the first warning of a faster unwind will come from Pittsburgh or Sandviken rather than from Bergisch Gladbach.

Reasoning.

What the disclosure answers well. The segment margins answer the question "is the fade on schedule" each quarter with a one-quarter lag; the order intake and backlog by segment answer "is the volume cycle turning" (Engineering 1.22x book-to-bill, backlog EUR 430m; Infrastructure orders +23% and backlog at a two-year high; Materials orders +44%, mostly price) and, because the Materials order line is a price series, it will be the first company figure to show deflation. The working-capital line on the company's definition (EUR 627m at June against EUR 461.5m at December) answers "is the cash releasing" at each quarter-end, and the leverage ratio answers "is the balance sheet within policy". The guidance architecture (three segment margin ranges, a group range, a cash-flow floor) means that any move in the ranges is itself a signal, and management's habit of changing guidance promptly (five changes in two years) makes the ranges informative rather than decorative.

What it does not answer, and why that matters more than usual. BETEK is about 51% of Materials Solutions revenue (NuWays, May 2026) and generated the whole of the segment's windfall, so the segment margin blends a subsidiary at 30%-plus with others at high single digits; a Q3 segment margin of 17.5% is consistent with BETEK anywhere from 25% to 30% and the non-tungsten units from 6% to 9%, and the two paths from there differ. The price-volume split has been given once ("about two-thirds price") and not updated. Management said inventory would be written down if prices fell abruptly but has given no threshold, no tonnage and no carrying value; the Q1 and H1 reports give total inventories (EUR 495m at March, EUR 557m at June) and nothing by segment. The AGM disclosure that major customers co-finance inventory (advance payments received EUR 34.5m, up from EUR 19.1m) is the single most important fact for the working-capital release and it came in a speech, not a table, with no terms; if those customers withdraw advances as prices fall, the release is slower and the company's quarterly figures will not say why. The 2030 target is given as revenue, EBITA, margin and EPS, but the inorganic bridge is not tracked publicly except at year-end (acquired revenue and EBITA against target, where the FY25 figure of EUR 1.9m against EUR 8 to 9m was disclosed only in the annual report).

The practical monitoring plan for six months, with the thresholds I would use. (i) 4 November, Kennametal fiscal Q1 2027: the Infrastructure segment's price-raw timing benefit against the USD 39m of the June quarter and its FY27 guidance of a mid-teens EBITDA margin exit; a benefit still above USD 20m says the Western plateau is holding, a collapse toward zero says the give-back has started. (ii) 12 November, INDUS Q3: Materials Solutions margin (above 20% plateau; 16 to 19% base; below 15% fast unwind), Engineering margin (above 8% says the Q4 ramp to 12.5% is credible; below 7% puts the FY26 8.5% at risk), working capital (below EUR 580m front-loaded release; EUR 600 to 630m base; above EUR 630m slow), nine-month free cash flow on the company's definition (positive is needed for the "at least break-even" year), the Materials order intake growth (a fall below 20% is the price series turning), any acquisition announced, any change to the ranges. (iii) Weekly: Fastmarkets or Argus Western APT and the SMM Chinese domestic price; the spread (about 40% at mid-October) is the measure of the Chinese finished-tool threat, and a Western price below USD 2,000 per MTU before year-end would put the EUR 5m allowance at risk of being EUR 15 to 20m. (iv) Monthly: VDMA order intake and the ifo expectations index for Engineering; German construction permits for Infrastructure's residential tail; Bundesbank data on the EUR 500bn fund's outflows (EUR 0.5bn in H1 2026). (v) February 2027 preliminaries and March 2027 annual report: the FY26 Materials margin against the 15 to 17% range, any allowance, acquired revenue and EBITA against target, the first FY27 ranges, and the tax rate. (vi) Early Q1 2027: the CME tungsten futures curve.

Against peers, INDUS's disclosure is better than Gesco's (half-yearly segment detail) and worse than Sandvik's (which discloses its powder and tool pricing lags, recycling share and timing benefit each quarter) and Kennametal's (which discloses the price-raw benefit by segment and its working-capital target). The company's investor-relations head changed in 2026 without comment, and the Q1 review's complaint that the cash trade-off was under-communicated stands.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into operational drivers, revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to EPS?

❌ D46

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement. The chain from guidance to EBITA is clear in structure and specific in its segment building blocks, but the one coefficient that governs the current year and the next (the transmission from the tungsten price to BETEK's margin) is not quantified by the company and has to be borrowed from peers, and the bridge from EBITA to EPS carries the group's largest historical forecasting error (a tax rate that has ranged from 28.7% to 43.1% in three years) plus a net financial line that has doubled since 2023. The external transmission is, by contrast, unusually legible for an industrial holding: one policy variable (Chinese export licensing) sets one price (Western APT) that moves one subsidiary's selling price at short intervals and its cost with a two-to-three-quarter lag, and each 10% of that price retained or lost is about EUR 10m of group adjusted EBITA and EUR 0.27 of EPS. The Consumer can therefore write a transmission map that is clear at every link and precise at none of the links the company controls.

Reasoning, link by link.

Company statements to operational drivers. FY26 guidance (revenue EUR 1.90 to 2.10bn; adjusted EBITA EUR 220 to 260m; Engineering 8.5 to 10.5%, Infrastructure 10 to 12%, Materials 15 to 17%; free cash flow at least break-even) traces into three operational drivers the company names: tungsten pricing and delayed cost pass-through at BETEK (Materials), back-end-loaded project completions and utilisation (Engineering), digital-network and grid demand against residential weakness (Infrastructure). The 2030 ambition traces into EUR 700m of organic revenue (about 7% a year, which the company does not decompose by segment) and EUR 600m of acquired revenue from EUR 500m of acquisitions (which it does decompose: EUR 20 to 100m targets, EUR 4m-plus of EBITA for first-tier deals, 5.5 to 6.5x historically, 7 to 7.5x recently). The organic bridge is the vaguest statement the company makes and the one the T-D1 report finds least plausible.

Operational drivers to revenue, cost and margin lines. Materials: price is "about two-thirds" of H1 growth; the margin went from 8.1% to 22.4% on a 32% revenue rise, a 66% drop-through; the reverse drop-through is implied but not stated. Engineering: revenue traces from backlog (EUR 430m, 2027 and 2028 deliveries) and the margin from utilisation (79% against 85% historical) and pricing pressure (NuWays' 1.5 point Q2 estimate), but the company gives neither utilisation by subsidiary nor the indexation of the backlog, so the T-I1 report's question "what proportion of the EUR 430m is protected" cannot be answered. Infrastructure: orders by product group are not given, so "digital-network demand" is a qualitative attribution to a 23% order rise and a 12.6% Q2 margin. Cost: the personnel ratio (31.9%, rising) and materials ratio (44 to 46%) are disclosed annually; the effect of 3% collective wage growth on EUR 554m of personnel cost (EUR 17m) is a straightforward calculation the company does not make for investors.

Margin to EPS. Adjusted EBITA less PPA amortisation (EUR 20.8m in FY25, disclosed annually, rising with deals) gives EBIT; less net financial items (EUR 25.3m net interest in FY25; EUR 13.1m in H1 2026; put-option revaluations on EUR 37.8m of minority commitments add noise) gives pre-tax; the tax rate is where the chain breaks: 41.8%, 43.1% and 28.7% in FY23 to FY25, 36% in H1 2026, with the structural cause (trade tax assessed at each limited partnership, so losses at loss-making subsidiaries cannot offset profits elsewhere: EUR 5.98m of extra tax in FY25, EUR 7.11m in FY24, EUR 10.22m in FY22) explained in the annual report but never guided. NuWays models 29%, the second set 34%; the difference is EUR 0.30 of FY27 EPS, which is a tenth of the figure. The 2028 corporate-tax reduction adds a non-cash deferred-tax revaluation of unknown timing (the company has EUR 39.7m of net deferred tax liabilities; FY25 already carried a EUR 4.13m positive effect from future rate changes). The share count is flat at 24.9m, so EPS tracks net income, which is the one clean link.

The external transmission. The value-chain map is: Chinese export licensing (February 2025; the 21 October 2026 reports of a ban on intermediates) sets Chinese export volumes; Western APT (USD 330 per MTU in early 2025, above USD 3,100 in August 2026, plateaued since) and Chinese domestic APT (down 69% from the March peak by mid-October) diverge by about 40%; BETEK buys Western feedstock under framework agreements it does not describe and sells wear tools to road-construction (Wirtgen and its dealers), mining and agricultural customers at prices adjusted "at short intervals"; its cost of goods carries inventory bought one to three quarters earlier; competitors (Ceratizit at 91% recycling with Sangdong offtake through Plansee; Sandvik with its own mine and 55% recycling; Chinese finished-tool makers at domestic feedstock cost; small European makers returning) set the price ceiling as supply normalises; Sangdong (commercial from September 2026, up to 40% of non-Chinese demand at Phase II) and Mitsubishi's Akita and H.C. Starck expansions set the Western supply path; the US DFARS rule from 1 January 2027 and the DLA's stockpile contract set a floor under traceable Western prices. The arithmetic through to EPS is clear: Materials Solutions revenue about EUR 757m in FY26 of which BETEK about half; each 10 points of segment margin is about EUR 75m of EBITA and EUR 2.0 of EPS; the fixed PPA and interest of about EUR 50m below EBITA turn a 27% EBITA fall into a 33% EPS fall. Nothing in this chain is obscure; what is missing is the company's own quantification of the two lags.

What would make the chain clear enough to forecast a quarter ahead: a BETEK revenue and volume disclosure, the share of Materials sales under index-linked contracts, inventory by segment with tonnage, the tax rate expected for the year, and the acquired-EBITA run rate. None of these is likely before the annual report in March 2027, and most not then.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically, and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

❌ D38

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement. Idiosyncratically, and increasingly so. Over the latest six-month window the share's daily returns have a correlation of 0.40 with the equal-weighted basket of its dashboard peers, a beta of 0.64 and an R-squared of 16%: 84% of the share's daily variance is its own. The share rose 35% in the window while the basket rose 5%, which is the tungsten windfall and the two guidance raises being priced, not a sector re-rating. The multiple tells the same story from the other side: INDUS's 24-month-forward P/E is at the 89th percentile of its own five-year range while its peers' P/E percentiles against their own ten-year histories are scattered (Aalberts 40, Lifco 53, Sdiptech 52, TKH 52, Storskogen 77, Volati 6, Idun 2, Rosebank 0), so there is no common sector re-rating for INDUS to have ridden. The multiple is therefore company-driven, and what the company has driven it with is a one-year earnings level that every source expects to fall. For predictability this is negative in a specific way: a share that decoupled upward on an idiosyncratic one-off will not be held up by the sector when the one-off reverses, and its multiple will be set by its own next three reports rather than by peers' results, rates or rotation.

Reasoning.

The co-movement data and its caveat. The 24 month-end readings of the rolling six-month window show two regimes: windows with correlation of 0.21 to 0.51 and R-squared of 5% to 26% (November 2024 to January 2025, August 2025 to January 2026, August to October 2026) and windows with correlation of 0.02 to 0.06 and beta of zero (February to July 2025 and February to July 2026). The second regime is almost certainly a data artefact (six of the fifteen peers have no price history in the file, and a beta of exactly 0.00 for six consecutive months is not a market outcome), so the Consumer should read the real series as correlation 0.2 to 0.5 and R-squared 5 to 26%, with the most recent window (0.40, 16%) in the middle of that band. Within the window, the highest pairwise correlation is with thyssenkrupp (0.45, R-squared 20%), another German industrial whose share has re-rated on an idiosyncratic story (its twelve-month return is 56%), then Aalberts and TKH (0.34 each), then the UK and Nordic names at 0.05 to 0.24. INDUS co-moves with German industrial sentiment more than with the diversified-holding model; the "serial acquirer" peers that the first set used as the quality benchmark (Lifco, Indutrade, Addtech, Sdiptech) are not in the price series, but their multiples (Lifco 26.7x, Sdiptech 13.5x) and their twelve-month returns (Lifco -4.8%, Sdiptech +42%) show no common move that INDUS could have followed.

Where the multiple sits and why that is company-specific. The 24-month-forward P/E was 8.54x on 16 October against a five-year median of 6.70x and a 90th percentile of 8.61x; the ten-year bands put today between the "prudent" median (8.9x) and the "conservative" 35th percentile (7.7x) because the ten-year history includes the 2017 to 2019 period when INDUS traded at 10 to 14x on a different earnings base. The dashboard's valuation rating is C (P/E percentile 45 on ten years). The multiple has expanded from about 6.7x to 8.5x in a year in which the 24-month-forward consensus EPS rose about 40%; the share has therefore captured both the earnings rise and a re-rating, which is the pattern the first set warned about (a record margin capitalised as a run rate). Peers show no equivalent: the sector digest's reading that the 2026 regime "refuses re-ratings to small acquirers" is borne out by Lifco at the 53rd percentile and Storskogen's and Volati's P/E percentiles being driven by their own earnings collapses rather than by sector moves.

What follows for the next 18 months. (i) The share will trade on the three INDUS reports (12 November, February, March) and on tungsten headlines, not on the sector; the Q3 reaction will be the first test, and the T-I2 and OWN-6 reports' finding that the share rose only 1.2% on the Q2 beat (against the SDAX's 0.2%) while NuWays has since downgraded on valuation suggests positive surprises are now less rewarded than negative ones are punished. (ii) Read-across risk runs one way: Sandvik, Kennametal and Almonty commentary on tungsten will move INDUS (the first set's C-82), but INDUS's results will not move them, and sector-wide good news (PMIs above 52, German fiscal conversion) will help the other 42 companies' volume cycle slowly without changing the BETEK path. (iii) The idiosyncratic share of variance means a peer-relative hedge would remove little of the risk; the risk is the stock's own. (iv) The fair-multiple question (topic #11) should be answered from the company's own range and phase, not from peers: the company is at the peak of a one-off earnings phase about to turn down, which is the phase in which the guideline says the multiple belongs in the lower half of its range, and the ten-year median of 8.9x is already above where the share has traded for most of five years. The guideline fair multiple of 14.26x in the model is the ten-year 90th percentile and corresponds to the 2017 to 2018 period when INDUS was a steady low-double-digit-margin compounder with positive organic growth; nothing in the next 18 months recreates that phase.

Back to contents

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: Assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D38 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Our assessment is reflected in company guidance to the extent that guidance exists, which is only for FY26, where we sit at the midpoint of a range that management set conservatively and that the October peer evidence says will be met in its upper half; it is not reflected in sell-side consensus for FY27 and FY28, which stands 13% above our EBITDA and 18% above our EPS on five models that have not been updated since the July guidance raise, and which carries no working-capital release in the year we expect EUR 180 to 210m of free cash flow; and it is reflected in the market narrative at first order only, because every covering house says the windfall is temporary and none has worked through what its financing, its repricing mechanics, the Chinese finished-tool channel, the structural tax rate or the succession mean for the shape and depth of the FY27 trough. The sequence over the next 18 months follows from this: FY26 guidance is confirmed or narrowed upward on 12 November and met in the upper half in February; the five stale brokers cut FY27 in two steps between November and March, the median converging on the two updated houses and then on an initial FY27 range that management will set at or below our base case; breadth falls from 86% buy as targets are reached and the revision-momentum rating turns from B toward D; and from April 2027 a low range, a visible cash release, resumed acquisitions and the H2 comparison base allow modest upgrades from a lower level, conditional on Western tungsten prices plateauing rather than collapsing. The gap between our assessment and what is priced is therefore unfavourable for a long case over the next six months (the market has capitalised an earnings level that guidance, consensus and the narrative all agree will fall, at a multiple at the 89th percentile of its five-year range, with the share's variance 84% idiosyncratic so that the sector will not cushion the adjustment) and roughly neutral over months six to eighteen (the cash and balance-sheet improvement is real, is partly known to the one broker that matters, and will be credited once the earnings cut has been taken). The one genuinely non-consensus positive we hold, that FY27 free cash flow will be about EUR 180 to 210m against a consensus EUR 131m, is worth about EUR 2 to 3 a share of net debt reduction and is not large enough to offset a multiple that, on our fair range of 7 to 9x and brokers'-basis EPS of about EUR 3.95 at December 2027, gives EUR 30 to 36 a share against a price of EUR 37.05. Richard should read this set as saying that the market knows the direction, has not priced the distance, and will be told the distance by the company and the sell-side between November 2026 and March 2027.

Guidance

1 question
I-0147
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

❌ D47

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement. The FY26 guidance will almost certainly be met and more likely than not in the upper half of its range; the probability of a further raise or an upward narrowing at the Q3 report on 12 November is about 20%, of confirmation about 70%, of a cut about 10%. The initial FY27 guidance, to be set in March 2027, will be set below the current consensus trajectory on earnings and above it on cash, with a probability I put at about 75%, because management has already told the market that margins will subside, because it sets initial ranges conservatively and raises after delivery, and because the March 2027 ranges will be set with the Q4 2026 Materials margin and any inventory allowance known. Once set low, FY27 guidance is more likely to be raised (about 35%, at the H1 2027 report in August 2027) than cut (about 20%) over the remainder of the window, which is the company's five-year pattern in every year that was not a German downturn surprise. The mid-term 2030 ambition will be reaffirmed rather than raised in March 2027 (the EPS target of above EUR 4.50 has already been exceeded in FY26 on every broker's figure, and the revenue target of about EUR 3bn is out of reach without an acquisition pace the company has not shown); the probability that the revenue and acquisition elements are quietly re-based or extended by 2028 is about 40%, and the probability of a raise to any 2030 element is below 15%. The gap between our assessment and guidance is therefore small in FY26 (we are at the midpoint; guidance is conservative; the direction of surprise within the year is up) and is not yet a gap in FY27 because no guidance exists; when it does, it will be set near our base case. That is neutral-to-mildly favourable for an investment case: guidance will not be the source of disappointment, but it will formalise the earnings decline the share has not yet priced.

Reasoning, event by event.

12 November 2026, Q3 report. What drives a raise or upward narrowing: a Q3 Materials Solutions margin above 20% (Sandvik's Q3 on 22 October still carried a positive timing benefit and its customers accepted September increases; Kennametal's benefit is front-loaded into the September and December quarters; Western APT has plateaued near USD 3,000 while only Chinese domestic prices have fallen), which would put nine-month adjusted EBITA near EUR 185m and make the EUR 240m midpoint look conservative; management raised after Q1 and after Q2 preliminaries when the numbers were in hand, so a Q3 margin of that kind could produce a narrowing to, say, EUR 235 to 255m. Against that, management's own H2 fade is its stated expectation, the Q4 is where the cost lag bites, and a sandbagger does not raise into a quarter it expects to be weaker, so the base case is confirmation with commentary that margins are "normalising as expected". What drives a cut: a Materials margin below 14%, a Q3 inventory allowance, or an Engineering margin below 7% with slipped Q4 completions; I put this at 10% because Western prices have not yet fallen and the cost lag protects Q3. The cash-flow guidance ("at least break-even") is the more exposed line: nine-month free cash flow on the company's definition needs to be clearly positive after H1's EUR -36.9m; the seasonal release and the Q2 inflection (EUR +37.2m) make that likely, and a Q3 working-capital balance below EUR 600m would allow management to say the floor is comfortably exceeded. Peer read-across before the day: Kennametal on 4 November (Infrastructure price-raw benefit and FY27 margin commentary), Krones' and Dürr's cuts already in (Engineering), Gesco's Q3.

February 2027, FY26 preliminaries. Probability FY26 adjusted EBITA lands above the EUR 240m midpoint: about 60%; above EUR 250m: about 25%; below EUR 230m: about 15%. The Materials full-year margin against the 15 to 17% range (the model has 18%) and any Q4 allowance decide it. Revenue will be near EUR 2.0bn. Free cash flow on the company's definition should be positive, probably EUR 40 to 90m, above the floor and above NuWays' EUR 32m if the H2 release is front-loaded.

March 2027, annual report and initial FY27 guidance. The likely form, by the company's habit (specific segment ranges, group EBITA range, cash floor, no EPS): revenue EUR 1.90 to 2.05bn; adjusted EBITA EUR 160 to 195m (margin 8 to 10%); Materials 8 to 11%; Engineering 8.5 to 10.5% again; Infrastructure 10 to 12% again; free cash flow well above FY26, perhaps "above EUR 100m". Consensus EBITDA for FY27 is EUR 292m (median) and EBIT EUR 180m, which implies adjusted EBITA of about EUR 200m; an initial range centred on EUR 175 to 180m would sit about 10% below the stale median and at the two updated brokers (NuWays EUR 179.8m; Metzler about EUR 190m) and at our base (EUR 174m). Probability that the initial FY27 EBITA range midpoint is below consensus EBIT plus PPA: about 75%; that it is below our EUR 174m: about 30% (management will want to leave room). The 2030 targets will be reaffirmed in the same document; a raise is improbable because the organic bridge is behind, and a formal cut is improbable because the company does not rebase targets three years early.

May 2027, Q1 2027. The hardest comparison is Q2, so Q1 will show the first year-on-year decline in Materials and group EBITA (the model's Q1 2027 adjusted EBITA is about EUR 35m against EUR 42.5m) but inside a range set to absorb it. Guidance maintained (80%); cut (15%) if Western prices have collapsed and the allowance is larger; raise (5%).

August 2027, H1 2027. The event at which the company's pattern says a raise comes if delivery allows. If Western prices have held above USD 2,000 per MTU, BETEK has retained more than half of its price and the working-capital release is on track, management will narrow upward (35%). If the Chinese finished-tool threat has taken volume and the H1 Materials margin is below 9%, a cut to the lower half (20%). Otherwise confirmation (45%).

November 2027, Q3 2027, and February 2028, FY27 preliminaries. Confirmation and delivery inside the range; the FY27 year will be judged on cash and leverage, which should be good (net debt toward EUR 570m, equity ratio at 40%), and on whether acquisitions have resumed at EUR 50m-plus. The FY28 guidance in March 2028 is outside the window.

The secondary question, 18 months to three years out. The 2030 ambition (revenue about EUR 3bn, adjusted EBITA above EUR 330m, 11% margin, EPS above EUR 4.50, EUR 500m of acquisitions adding EUR 600m of revenue) has three elements with different fates. The EPS element has been met early by the windfall and will be met again on the model's path only in FY30 after adding back PPA (EUR 4.64 on the brokers' basis); the company will not raise it while the FY27 trough is in view. The margin element (11%) is the T-D1 report's "achievable" element and NuWays agrees; on the model it is the ceiling (9.7% in FY30), so by 2028 management will either be visibly short or will have re-based through mix; probability of being met by 2030: about 30%. The revenue and acquisition elements require EUR 100m-plus a year of deals from 2027; the model has EUR 58m, 75m, 85m and 95m; the company said on 5 October that further acquisitions are expected before year-end 2026, and NuWays expects deal flow to accelerate, but the FY25 acquired-EBITA shortfall (EUR 1.9m against EUR 8 to 9m) and the first set's record say the pace will trail; probability that the EUR 3bn target is met: about 15%; probability of an extension or re-basing by the FY27 annual report: about 40%. The CEO's contract ends on 31 December 2027; a successor's first act often includes a target review, which raises the re-basing probability beyond the window.

Why the gap scores only slightly favourable. Our FY26 base is at guidance's midpoint and the evidence favours the upper half: a small favourable gap. Our FY27 base is where guidance will be set: no gap once set, and the setting itself is the negative event for the share. Our 2030 view is below the targets: an unfavourable gap that is already partly discounted by the sell-side (NuWays models a 4.3% organic CAGR against the implied 5.9%) and will not bite within 18 months.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0233
I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

❌ D33

Judgement. Consensus earnings for FY27 and FY28 will be cut, consensus cash flow for FY27 will be raised, and the net breadth of revisions will turn negative on earnings between the Q3 report on 12 November 2026 and the March 2027 annual report, with a probability I put at about 80% for a FY27 EBITDA cut of 5 to 12% (from EUR 292m toward EUR 255 to 275m) and a FY27 EPS cut of 8 to 18% (from EUR 3.94 toward EUR 3.2 to 3.65) by March 2027. The mechanism is already in motion: five of the seven brokers last updated in July or August on the strength of the guidance raise and before the October conference, and the two that have updated since (NuWays, 6 October, EUR 3.63; Metzler, 16 October, EUR 3.65) sit 7 to 8% below the median they are part of; when the other five roll their models after Q3 and again after the FY26 results and the March guidance, the median converges on the updated pair and then on the guidance. FY26 EPS consensus (EUR 5.21) is also likely to be trimmed by about 5% at the February preliminaries on tax and interest even if EBITA lands in the upper half, because the median embeds a tax rate near 29% that only the FY25 one-off produced. From about April 2027 the direction reverses: an initial FY27 range set low, a cash flow that beats on the working-capital release, resumed acquisition announcements (which enter estimates only when closed) and the H2 2027 comparison base make modest upgrades to FY27 and FY28 estimates the more likely outcome in the second half of the window, from a lower base. The gap between our assessment and consensus is therefore unfavourable on earnings for the next six months (we are 13% below the FY27 EBITDA median and 18% below the EPS median) and favourable on cash (we are about 40% above the FY27 free-cash-flow median on the FactSet basis), and the earnings cut will arrive before the cash upgrade is believed.

Reasoning.

Where consensus sits against us and against the updated brokers. FY26: revenue EUR 2,016m (ours 2,004), EBITDA EUR 315m (ours 315), EBIT EUR 215m (ours, adjusted EBITA less PPA, about EUR 217m), EPS EUR 5.21 (ours 4.85 on the brokers' basis; the range runs from MP Capital's and Parmantier's EUR 4.3 to 4.4 to NuWays', Oddo's and Pareto's EUR 5.5); free cash flow EUR 32m (ours about 28 before leases). FY27: revenue EUR 2,043m (ours 1,962; Pareto 1,884; NuWays 2,034), EBITDA EUR 292m (ours 255; Pareto 247; NuWays 263; Metzler 272; Oddo 310), EPS EUR 3.94 (ours 3.22; NuWays 3.63; Metzler 3.65; Oddo 4.99), free cash flow EUR 131m (ours 182 after leases, 211 before). FY28: revenue EUR 2,158m (ours 2,070), EBITDA EUR 290m (ours 278), EPS EUR 4.15 (ours 3.73). The consensus FY27 operating cash flow of EUR 206m against EBITDA of EUR 292m less about EUR 74m of tax and interest embeds no working-capital release; NuWays' own model embeds EUR 104m in H2 2026 and EUR 39m in FY27, so the median's cash line is stale in the opposite direction from its earnings line.

The revision history and what it says about the next move. FY27 EPS consensus is up 9% over three months, 14% over six and 15% over twelve; the target price mean is up 17% and 26%; the dashboard's revision-momentum score is 11 and its rating B; the buy share is 86% after one downgrade (NuWays to Hold on 6 October on valuation) that took it down 14 points in a month; the mean target is EUR 42.3 and the median EUR 41.0 against a price of EUR 37.05, with Pareto's target (EUR 37) at the price and MP Capital's (EUR 53) the outlier. Every component of this picture is at or near its high: estimates, targets, breadth and momentum. The first set's C-74 called the setup "the wrong end" for a contrarian case, and nothing since has changed that; the one-month EPS revision of +1% and EBITDA revision of 0% show the upgrade cycle has stopped, and the next move from a stop at a high is down.

The sequence of events and the likely revisions. (i) 12 November: the five stale brokers update. If Q3 Materials is at or above the Analyst's 17.5%, FY26 estimates hold or rise slightly and FY27 estimates are cut only modestly as the brokers adopt management's fade language; if below 15%, both years are cut. Net breadth turns from flat to negative on FY27 either way, because the two updated brokers are already below the five. Expected median FY27 EBITDA after Q3: about EUR 275 to 285m. (ii) February 2027 preliminaries: FY26 EPS trimmed on tax; FY27 estimates adjusted for any Q4 allowance. (iii) March 2027 annual report and FY27 guidance: the decisive cut. A range centred on EUR 175 to 180m of adjusted EBITA takes the FY27 EBITDA median to about EUR 255 to 265m and the EPS median to about EUR 3.3 to 3.6; FY28 follows down by a similar proportion because brokers roll the lower base forward. The cash line is raised at the same time if the FY26 cash flow and year-end working capital show the release (NuWays' template), and the two most recent notes already use the FY27 free-cash-flow yield as the valuation anchor, so the sell-side will frame the year as "earnings trough, cash peak" and hold target prices rather than cut them in proportion to EPS. Ratings: one or two further moves to Hold are likely once the price is at or through targets (Pareto is there), taking the buy share toward 60 to 70%. (iv) May to August 2027: estimates stabilise; the August H1 report is where the sandbag-and-raise pattern would start upgrades to FY27 and FY28 if prices have plateaued; acquisitions announced in Q4 2026 and H1 2027 add 1 to 2% to FY27 and FY28 revenue estimates as they close. (v) November 2027: FY28 becomes the valuation year; estimates for FY28 (then EUR 3.7 to 4.0 of EPS) begin to carry the acquisition contributions and the lower tax rate.

Why the cut is highly probable and the subsequent upgrade only moderately so. The cut requires nothing but time: the stale models embed a Materials margin that management itself has said will subside, and the two brokers closest to the company have already cut. The upgrade requires one of three things to go right: Western prices holding (the plateau branch, about 25%), acquisitions resuming at EUR 50m-plus a year (which NuWays expects and which management signalled on 5 October), or the Engineering backlog converting at margin in 2027 (medium plausibility). I put the probability that FY27 consensus EPS in October 2027 is above where it stands after the March 2027 cut at about 50%, and above today's EUR 3.94 at about 15%.

The secondary question. Consensus for FY28 and FY29 (EPS EUR 4.15 for FY28; one broker at EUR 275m of EBITDA for FY29) will be cut with FY27 in March 2027 and then rebuilt from a lower base through 2027 and 2028 as acquisitions close and the 2028 tax cut enters models (NuWays still assumes 29% flat, so the tax cut is not an upgrade source for it; it is for the brokers at 32 to 34%). The sector digest's rule that consensus models only owned businesses, so a repaired balance sheet buying again is a stream of unpriced upgrades for 12 to 24 months, applies from mid-2027; it is the most credible source of outer-year upgrades and it depends on the engine restarting at a pace the company has not shown. Probability of outer-year upgrades net of the March cut by the end of the window: about 40%.

Why the gap scores unfavourably. We are below consensus on the line the market prices (EPS) by 18% in FY27 and 10% in FY28; the correction will come in two steps inside six months; breadth and momentum are at highs; the cash gap in our favour will be credited only after the earnings cut has been taken. The second half of the window is better, but from a lower base and on conditions.
